definitely looked solid and "round 98% of the time; it was

riving to Maidenhead to spend a

weekend with his fiancee in 1979,

Graham Allen had an experience that
changed his life. It was a further eight years
before he even began to understand it, and he
has no way of proving that it really happened.
But he believes beyond a shadow of a doubt
that it did, and that it has potentially world-
shaking implications for the rest of us.

Before we look at his story
in detail, it’s important to
establish that Graham is
no wild-eyed loon with a
direct line to the planet
Venus. A painter and
decorator in his thirties
with a wife and two
children, over a pintina
Brocton pub he comes
acroas as the very essence
of aoftly-apoken normality.

He does, however, claim
to have experienced a real-
life Close Encounter of the
Third Kind, and to have
seen UFOs of all shapes
and sizes in the vicinity of
his Rugeley home.

First things first,
though. The close
encounter.

“It was June and I wag
driving down to
Maidenhead to see my
fiancee for the weekend,”
he recalls. “We took it in
turns to visit and it was
my turn this time.

“I waa on the Oxford
ring road and I rounded
the traffic island to take
the turning] always took
for the A423, I know it
was about five to six
Deenuse there was a
timecheck on the radio.

“Moments after, though,
the radio died down, andI
thought I must be passing
under overhead cables or
something. I remember
leaning forward briefly to
adjust the radio, and when
Tlooked up again it was
raining, which didn’t
surprise me too much
because it had been warm
and hazy.

“[ drove on for about
five minutes more when it
struck me that I wasn’t
familiar with the road any
more. There was a signpost
which said Newby three
miles, at which point it
struck me that I'd gone
way past my turning.

Strange

“T pulled into a farm
entrance and felt very
strange. Sitting there I
noticed neither the car nor
the road were wet, and the
radio was playing again. It
was still only five minutes
past six, yet driving back
to rejoin the dual
carriageway at the turning
I'd missed ] found I'd been
20 miles off course.
Somehow I'd travelled 20
miles in two or three
minutes.

“When leventually

thought it was hilarious!"

At this stave, Graham
emphasises, there was
nothing to link his odd

Vivid

METEORIC hopes fell to earth at Prestwick last week
when an object thought to have come from outer space

turned out to be a pebble.

The drama started with a suspected air crash which changed
toa UFO alert before excited scientists investigated the streaks

of white light seen from the control tower.

However, their direanis
were dashed when the green
substance on the shuttered
rock recovered from the
airfield turned out to be
common lichen, and scorch
marks from outer space were
in fact tar deposits.

Air traffie controller Terry
Murphy was Supervising a
colleague at 8.30pm last Monday.
“Suddenly she gave a pasp of
amazement. Looking out of the
window a white ight was hurtling
fowirds the ground fron. $Gut1

oThe reason i startled us wits
peragt

that it was just like an a
diving towards the ground.

All aircraft in the area could be
accounted for and the incident
was logged as an untdentifind
flying object.

But on Tuesday morning a
ground crew member found a hole
punched in the north runway with
a piece of apparently scorched,
Silicate-type oval stone 3in long.
shattered into four Interltnking
pieces embedded in and around it,

Dr David Clarke, director of
Glasgow University Observatory,
was called in. Initially convinced
he was witnessing history in the
making only three such
discoveries have been made in

CHASE POST
wolverhampton

50 SEP 1993

Driving to Maidenhead to spend a weekend with his
fiancee in 1979, Graham Allen had an experience that
changed his life. [t was a further eight years before he
even began to understand it, and Te as no way of
proving that it really happened. But he believes beyond
a shadow of a doubt that it did, and that it has
potentially world-shaking implications for the rest of us.

Before we look at his story in detail, it’s important to
establish that Graham is no wild-eyed loon with a direct
line to the planet Venus. A painter and decorator in his
thirties with a wife and two kids, over a pint in a
Brocton pub he comes across as the very essence of
softly-spoken normality.

He does, however, claim to have been abducted by
apace aliens, and to have seen UFOs of all shapes and
Sizes in the vicinity of his Rugeley home. -

Strange encounter

First things first, though. The abduction.

“It was June and I was driving down to Maidenhead
to see my fiancee for the weekend,” he recalls. “We
took it in turns to visit and it was my turn this time.

“I was on the Oxford ring road and I rounded the
traffic island to take the turning I always took for the
A423. I know it was about five to six because there was
a timecheck on the radio.

“Moments after, though, the radio died down, and [
thought I must le passing under overhead cables or
something. I remember leaning forward briefly to
adjust the radio, and when.I looked up again it was
raining, which didn’t surprise me too much because it
had been warm and hazy.

“I drove on for about five minutes more when it
struck me that I wasn’t familiar with the road any
more. There was a signpost which said Newby three
miles, at which point it struck me that I'd gone way
past my turning. —

“T pulled into a farm entrance and felt very strange.
Sitting there 1 noticed neither the car nor the road
were wet, and the radio was playing again. It was still
only three minutes to six, yet driving back to rejoin the
dual carriageway at the turning I'd missed I found I'd
been 20 miles off course. Somehow I'd travelled 20
miles in two or three minutes.

“When I eventually arrived at m
was shaking all over and my legs
My fiancee thought it was hilarious!”

Disbelief

At this aluge, Graham umphasises, there wus nothing
to link his odd experience with UFOs. He didn’t even
believe in them. In the years that followed, however,
during the night he frequently experienced strange,
unsettling buzzing noises and inexplicable feelings of
losing control, until in 1987 he had an experience that
seemed to shed some light on the events of eight years
earlier.

“It was just before Christmas when in a vivid,
dreamlike state one night I relived my experience of
1979 from the point where the radio died down. The car
was stationary and surrounded by golden light. I was
confused, I had no idea what was going on and | could
hear a low humming sound. Across the road I saw a
man walking a dog.

“I looked up to see a brilliant light above the car. I
panicked and tried to get the electric window to wind
up, but it wouldn’t work. Then I heard a high pitched
sound, lost control and blacked out.

“When I came to I was lying down and there were
three inhuman faces locking down at me. My first
thought was that they must be wearing joke masks,

fiancee’s house, I
ad turned to jelly.

' Story: CHRIS es 14s
ere

but the intensity in their eyes suggested they

real. There was no emotion at all in the faces, They
were hairless with round dark eyes - pools of black -
narrow mouths and greyish skin. I couldn't move, I was
paralysed. Then I woke up.”

Nightmare

When Graham woke up, though, it was to find that
the nightmare had somehow spilled over into reality.

“It was about 3am and there was a low humming
noise over the house. My two children were woken by it
and one was crying, and my wife was awake too. i sent
her over to the window to see what it was - I wasn’t
about to take any chances after the dream I'd had! But
I needn't have worried as there was nothing to be
seen.”

Despite the apparent anti-climax, the night of
Graham's dream seemed to spark off what's known in
UFO circles as a flap, a sudden spate of sightings
centring around the Rugeley and Stafford area. In the
months that followed, local newspapers carried a flood
of reports, culminating in Stafford MP Bill Cash
raising the matter in the House of Commons.

_ Graham, too, became accustomed to seeing strange
lights in the sky, but for sheer Close Encounters drama
his experience of October 2, 1989 took the biscuit.

“I was walking on Etching Hill with the children -
around 7.20pm when we saw a bright light in the sky.
As we watched, it divided into two separate lights and
a spark suddenly shot off in the direction of Stafford.

“The two lights began to come towards us, like car
headlights. As they came closer it became clear that
they were part of a massive triangular object, covered
in lights, and looking like something out of Star Wars.”
I was crouched dewn with my arms around the
children, who were very frightened, as the object just
glided silently over us.

“Lookin; we saw a rectangular orange section
towards the back, and if there had been someone
waving at us we'd have been close enough to see them.
Then it just drifted off over Rugeley power station.

“When we got home I told the children to remember
everything they'd seen and to paint pictures of the
object, which they did.”

Your decision

Jn the years that followed, Graham began by keepin
quiet over, his experiences for fear of ridicule. He ati
had a business to run,.after all. But a year ago he
decided to speak out.

“| don’t know whut these things are or where they
come from. I know what I've seen exists beyond any
doubt whatsoever, and my experiences have altered the
way I look at life. I now know there’s a lot more to it
than meets the eye, but in the talks I give when asked,
I don't have any great message for mankind. I leave
people to make their own mind up over what it all
means.

“I just see my role a3 an amateur investigator and as
helping to prepare people for the culture shock they
might face one day. Because anyone who sees one of
these things is left with one big question mark over
their life.”

As we drained our pints and headed for the car park,
I had just one more question for Graham. His surname,
how was it spelt? -

“A-I-l-e-n,” he said, adding with a smile, “no i.”

RECENTLY I read a maga-
zine article which briefly
described a sighting of the
Loch Ness Monster.

Macinnes, who said that the
monster’s head was moving
backwards and forwards while
its neck was moving up and
down.

The article made the supposed
sighting seem like a load of non-
sense, giving it the title of The
Monster Mash?

‘How can
anyone
be sure?’

I'm not saying I believe the
famous Scottish myth but the
water's so deep, so how can
anyone be sure what's down
there?

Put it down to stories and folk
tales, but there’s always that
small doubt as to whether there
is any truth behind the tales.

meteorite

AIR TRAFFIC controllers at
Prestwick who thought they
were witnessing an air crash
may possibly have been
watching the first meteorite
to fall in the area for more
than 150 years.

Senior air traffic controller
Terry Murphy yesterday
said, “I was supervising
another controller when
suddenly she gave a gasp of
amazement. Looking out of
the window a white light was
hurtling towards the ground
from 300 feet.

“The reason it startled us
was that it was just like an
aircraft diving towards the
ground. We had aircraft fly-
ing at the time and we
classified it as an unidenti-
fied flying object.” The inci-
dent, which happened at 8.30

on Monday night was re-.

ported to both UX air traffic

control centres at Prestwick -

and London and the Ministry
of Defence, who made no
comment.

But on Tuesday moming a
ground crew worker re-
ported that a hole had
appeared on one of the
tarmac and concrete aprons
on the north side of the
airfield.

Staff found a rock inside '
the hole and another piece ;

next to it. Two other frag-
ments were discovered
nearby and when all four
pieces were put together
they formed a clearly
scorched object seven centi-
metres long by five centi-
metres wide and weighing
around 100 grammes.

Mr Murphy added, “The
Meteorological Research
Flight from Farnborough,

which was here on a special .

task investigating clouds, got
very excited about this and
said we should be contacting
a university astronomy
department.”

Dr David Clarke, director
of Glasgow University obser-
vatory, examined the site
yesterday and revealed no

. other meteorite had beén

found in the area since 1839.

He estimated it to be bil-
lions of years old and said
that, while sightings were
not uncommon, it was very
unusual for a meteorite to be
seen to fall and be recovered
in such a short period of
time. However, another
scientist last night said he
thought the main fragment
was probably just a pebble.

Section 40 Date

22 September 1993
Var

Thank you for sending me a copy of Working Paper 12/93 which concerns UFO
sightings in the Ramsey area.

I received the basic details esaSection 40) | sighting shortly after the first
report was made, and have already written to him, setting out the MOD’s policy
and views on UFOs. I received one further report from the Ramsey area; although
the details were vague, the sighting occurred in the early hours of 4 August.

I agree with your conclusion that helicopter activity seems to be the most
likely explanation. We asked RAF Alconbury whether or not they were operating
helicopters in the Benwick area at the times concerned, but they have advised
that they were not. Of course, it may well be that some other helicopter (such
as a police helicopter) was involved.

I thought it might be useful if I explained our role in relation to UFO
sightings; essentially, our only reason for being involved at all is to ensure
that there is no threat to the defence of the UK. We, together with other
specialist staffs, look at all UFO reports carefully, to look for evidence of
any such threat. To date we have found no such evidence. Most of the reports
we receive are fairly vague, and refer to little more than a vague light or
shape in the sky. Clearly there is little we can do with such reports. Where a
report is more interesting (either because of the description of the object and
its behaviour, or in cases where many witnesses are involved) we will make
further enquiries. You stated that you would normally expect JARIC to action
such a case; while I know that they could be asked to examine any interesting
UFO photographs that we may be sent, I was not aware that they had a day-to-day
role in investigating sightings. Perhaps you would let me know what the
position is.

Although I do not believe we can take any further action with regard to these
specific sightings, your report has highlighted some confusion about how UFO
reports should be handled. RAF bases, police stations and civil airports should
all have standard forms for the reporting of sightings, but the system does not
work as well as it might. This is clearly illustrated by the fact that RAP
Wyton assumed the report to be a crank call, and cut Bee off. We intend to
review the existing system, and promulgate advice on the handling of UFO
reports.

REPORTED SIGHTING, ACTIONS AND SUGGESTIONS
Introduction
1. This note records the reporting of UFO activity in the Ramsey area of Cambridge in Jul and

Aug 93. The report was actioned through official channels and formal acknowledgement of the
sighting has been made to the principal individual involved. Some possible explanations based on the
descriptions of the sighting are made.

Initial Report

2. On the morning of the 26 Jul 93 an attempt was made to report, over the telephone to RAF
Wyton, a UFO sighting. The operator took it to be a crank/hoax call and terminated the contact. The
caller then phoned RAF Brampton where the operator accepted the call and used the copy facility so
that a tape of the conversation was available for analysis.

3. The telephone report was being made, at 08:15 on the morning of 26 Jul 93, by a BERS of
Ramsey, Cambridgeshire, on behalf of his father who was not available to make the call himself. The
Teport can be summarised as follows:

Section 4CRemte is not in the best of health so that he often wakes in the night. In the early
hours of the morning of 26 Jul 93 he was awake and his attention was drawn to a bright object
in the night sky seemingly a few miles to the NNE of his home in Ramsey, in the vicinity of the
fen village of Benwick. He then found his ‘high powered German’ binoculars and focused them
on the light. For the next hour or so, accompanied by his wife, he observed the mainship
hovering. Initially 2 smaller ships came down. Little lights around the spaceship were seen.’

A full transcript of the telephone report made Peasection 4Fa at Annex A.

4. After hearing the tape of this initial call [ fem oection 4] junior back to ask if he had anything
else to add and volunteered to process the inquiry. (Note that action would probably have been
requested of JARIC staff but in their absence, on stand down, the inquiry was picked up by Research
Branch, HQRAFSC.) I contacted the radar unit at RAF Wyton to see if they had any record of
anything unusual some 12-15 miles N-NNE of their location. Unfortunately the radar was not manned
at the time (03:15 to 04:45 on 26 Jul 93) so I was given a West Drayton contact. Eastern Radar of
London ATC took details from me and said they would action it through official MOD channels.

bo

a

@ On the morning of the 27 Jul 93 I spoke by telephone with senior and explained the

ion taken. He confirmed the original details except that his version was that “2 things shot out of the
side of it" when first seen and described it as "a saucer with lattice work". He then went on to claim
that whilst in the local doctors’ waiting room on the evening of the 26th he discussed the sighting with
an elderly farm worker who was also waiting. Apparently this individual was not surprised since he
said that an object had come down to within some 50' of him whilst he was working in Pidley village,
back in 1983.

6. The map at Figure 1 shows Ramsey, where the sighting was made, and the village of Benwick,
the nearest habitation other than individual farms, in the area that the UFO was thought to be hovering

over.

Subsequent Report

7. One week later, 2 Aug 93, SERene junior phoned me to say that the sighting had been
repeated in the early hours of that morning and this time witnesses had confirmed the observation. A
possible explanation was discussed but rejected by gunior. Section 4a had received
official acknowledgement of his sighting on the 26th, the letter apparently clearing him to take
whatever follow up action he desired.

Possible Explanation

8. The initial description of an illuminated object hovering for an hour plus did seem difficult to
match with any man-made flying object especially with the claimed saucer shape as seen through
binoculars. However, there is a possible answer, as follows:-

The American Air Force operate out of RAF Alconbury some 15 to 20 miles south west of the
area of the sighting (see Figure 1). A current type operated is the Sikorsky HH-53 helicopter.
The range of this large rescue helicopter can be extended by external fuel tanks. Because of its
rescue role there are lights carried and hovering over an accident/incident site is a basic
requirement.

9. Consider the situation of flight training in the rescue role. The requirement is for long duration
in the hover. Given the extra tanks that can be fitted the idea of hovering for an hour plus is probably
feasible. Any combination of struts by which external tanks are attached, the nose flight refuelling
probe and the main and tail rotors could explain the ‘lattice work' comment. At night the combination
of navigation and cabin lighting would make it show up. Given the clear but windy weather in the early
hours of the 26th the hover would not be stable and rotation, either about the single main rotor axis or
tight circling to stay over the planned ground position means that the fuselage would sweep out a shape
other than the actual helicopter fuselage shape (see Figure 2). Use of searchlights could have been
seen, at some distance, as ‘projections’ from the craft. The sighting was over an area of low population
density possibly to minimise noise complaints and with the windy conditions the noise may well have
been attenuated anyway.

10. The implication that the first sighting disappeared and the second involved a rapid vertical
ascent to go out of sight are difficult to explain, but not impossible. If, in the first case, the helicopter
departed the area heading directly away from the observers the visible lights would be minimal. In the
second case the helicopter could have used its not inconsiderable climb rate, 1000 feet per minute plus
and if it went up into the cloud base the sudden disappearance might make it seem faster than it was.

}. The helicopter solution to this sighting can be resolved if the forces at Alconbury are prepared
admit to such training. Alternatively, the evidence of people on the ground at some of those isolated
fenland farms could probably confirm the noise from a very large hovering helicopter which would be

quite noticeable even if it was at a considerable height above the ground.

Conclusions

12. The coincidence of this UFO sighting happening close to a base operating the large helicopters
that could produce this impression in the sky really means its interpretation as a UFO is unlikely. Only
if the American forces deny any such operation and no one can be found to confirm the noise from such
a deployment, should this incident be taken as a truly Unidentified’ flying object.

INVESTIGATION REPORT - CASE NO: 938

Case Name or Reference ‘ Taunton / St Agnes Sightings’
(August 1993)

Introduction

Although two days apart both these sightings are so similar in nature and
description that there is undoubtedly a connection between them.

Both concern the sighting of 'two cigar shaped objects‘ traversing the
skies above Taunton, Somerset and St Agnes, Cornwall, in what appears to
be an identical flight pattern.

The first sighting was personally reported to me by KERIO who
lives in Taunton. is an employee in one of the shops, within the
shopping centre that I manage in Taunton.

Knowing of my interest in ufos, she related her sighting to me,
appreciating that if nothing else she would not be ridiculed or thought
of as a fool etc. I have known Reyuea for the best part of three years and
have always considered her to be an honest and trustworthy person.
because of this, I believe her sighting to be quite genuine, and can
actually corroborate part of her report myself - which is as follows:-

At approx 8.15am on Saturday 7th August 1993, Section Be standing at a bus
stop near her home, awaiting her bus to take her to work. She was alone
and on looking up at the sky noticed a rather extraordinary number of
aircraft vapour trails. Although it is not unusual to see vapour trails,
it did seem to her that there was rather a lot of them and more
importantly they appeared to be criss-crossing the sky - every which way.
Their configuration reminded her of a ball of wool having been undone and

/2

(2)

left untidily laying about. Whilst looking at them she noticed an
aircraft travelling northwards and heading away from Taunton. As the
aircraft disappeared from sight, she then saw two cigar shaped objects,
one following the other, moving very slowly across the sky in a northerly
direction. Because of their speed she was able to watch them for nearly
five minutes. She described them as silver in colour and reflecting the
early morning sun. They appeared to be about 30' apart and at a height
slightly lower than the aircraft previously seen. She estimated each
object to be about 1%" in length - at arms length. They were at an
elevation of about 70° to the horizon, but is unable to judge
their distance from her. At approx 8.20am BERWegRW bus arrived and she
then lost sight of the objects.

On this particular morning, I arrived in Taunton at approx 7.55am and
whilst parking my car, I happened to look up at the sky and noticed the
rather large number of vapour trails. Being in the west,, the sky above
Taunton, particularly on saturdays, does get very busy and vapour trails
are not uncommon. However, on this morning there was an extraordinary
number of them and were as I would expect them to be mainly from east to
west ( west to east), there were a number that criss-crossed the sky and
indeed some seemed to have approached from one direction and joined then
followed some others. The impression I got, was that a number of
aircraft had converged on one another and then headed away northwards!

The second sighting came from a police officer - Bfemegele

serves with the Devon & Cornwall Police Force. At Ipm on Tuesday 10th
August 1993, Fiance)and her sister BERGEN, were
driving along the B3285 east of Goonhavern, Cornwall, when they noticed
to the S.W. two objects flying or moving S.Easterly. They appeared to be
about 5 or 6 miles away and in line, one behind the other, moving very
slowly and appeared to be on a predetermined course. Beige described
them as silver / white in colour and at arms length about the

/3

(3)

size of a cigarette. She estimated that they were between 45° to 60° to
the horizon and that they ( B@geland her sister) had the objects in
sight for for about ten minutes. This obviously indicates a fairly slow
speed, and if estimate of the objects lengths are correct, then
they must have been fairly large!

Investigation and Conclusions

Because of my very heavy work load (Business and UFOs), I have not been
able to investigate these sightings in any great depth.

_ Fortunately, because of my close work association with Beem I
have been able to interview her firsthand. From her report and from what
she has told me of the event, I feel that what she saw on this morning
was certainly out of the ordinary. She is, I'm sure, able to identify
aircraft when she sees them and other natural aerial objects such as -
Hot Air Balioons, Helicopters, Gliders etc. Given the distance of the
objects from her, it is conceivable that they may have been some type of
aircraft! If they were, they would most probably have been some

military type flying in formation and also quite possibly carrying out a
re-fueling operation! This is of course merely speculation on my part,

but could I believe be one explanation.

What concerns me or rather I should say intrigues me, is the
extraordinary number of vapour trails that morning. Only last week, I
received a report of the sighting of a single cigar shaped object over
Bristol on Saturday 4th September 1993. The object was travelling very
fast in an easterly direction and a few seconds after being seen, a
number of fighter aircraft (7 or 8) were also seen. The jets arrived from
all compass points and converged east of Bristol and appeared to be
chasing the Ufo! Inquiries to the MOD (Air) of course produced no
results, but this is being followed up by letter. (Case No 9313 to
follow, refers).

/4
(4)
Both ladies are adamant that there were no ‘wings' or other ‘appendages'
affixed or associated with these objects, which does indicate that they
were not any known conventional aircraft. Given the spate of ufo
sightings in the west county over the last few months, it is reasonable
to assume that these objects may have been ufos of some description or
other.
Although all the objects were ‘cigar shaped' there is of course the
possibility that they were in fact ‘disc shaped’ and seen from an angle
or side view. Again this is speculation, but as the object seen over
Bristol on the 4th September was observed in a ‘vertical’ mode and not
‘horizontal’, then all things are possible.

In conclusion, although I have to say that there is the possibility that
these objects may have been aircraft, and if so, probably military, I do
feel that what was seen on these two days and also on the 4th September
over Bristol, were definitely unidentifiable by the sighters, and must,

for the time being anyway, be classified as ‘unknowns’.

INVESTIGATION REPORT Case No: 935 & 935A

Case Name or Reference; ‘Axminster & Packsaddle Sightings'

Introduction

Although the following reports occurred on different dates they
are considered to be sufficiently similar in nature to warrant
them as a dual event, possible of the same object.

The first event (Case No 935) occurred on the 14th May 1993 and

was reported by Seems lives at ifeieagite)
Section 40 Axminster, Devon. ESRC 0) as

Section 40 Axminster. Because of its
location; the rear and only garden of the property does not
afford its occupants a very good view of the surrounding area.
At 10.40pm on the day in question, had gone out in the
garden to smoke a cigarette and upon looking up noticed a
bright light approaching from the S.W. He estimated its height
to be between 10,000' and 20,000' and at an elevation of about
75 degrees to the horizon. On its approach Be@Qeg describes its
speed as faster than a jet aircraft and on a consistent course.
As the object reached a position right above his garden - it
suddenly stopped. This action rather startled as
although he was unable to identify the object he really hadn't
considered it to be anything other than perhaps a high flying
aircraft or even possibly a satellite.

The object remained stationary for for a few seconds, then
performed a forward motion going into a anti- clockwise circle

and returning to its former position.

/2

(2)
After another slight pause, the object then shot forward approx
2 or 3 miles and again stopped abruptly. It then performed
another circular motion stopping at its second position and
after a short pause returned to its approx first position over
the garden. At this point it once again performed another
circular motion and again stopped dead. It then moved forward
very fast and disappeared towards the S.E.- ascending very
fast. During its flight from its first stationary position to
its second Site <alled to his wife Section 40 [Ruan came out and
witnessed the second and final movements of the object and its
departure.
Both witnesses described the object as about the size of a new
five pence piece at arms length and agreed that the object
appeared oval in shape and although described as a bright self
luminous object appeared to be tinged with an orange / reddish
colour. Neither REQRROMMMENN could identify the object, but
they are certain it was not an aircraft or a helicopter.
In all the sighting lasted approx 1% minutes and needless to
say left both witnesses more than a little puzzled.

The second event (Case no 935'A') occurred on the 6th July 1993
and was reported by Seite) lives at Bite)
Ma Section 40 Although situated amongst a

At 11,18pb on the night, Ray (just as aid at Axminster)
went out into his garden for a smoke. Suddenly he saw an object
appear from over the top of his house and moving at a high
speed. it was ‘Ice Blue’ in colour and again was described as
about the size of a new pence piece at arms length. As
watched the object, it suddenly stopped in mid air and remained
stationary for a few seconds. It then moved forward in a zig-
zag motion and then shot forward and upwards at a tremendous

to his son Steven and a friend who both
witnessed the objects departure. Whilst stationary iSectif

but on its departure ascended towards the moon!

As in the Axminster sighting, none of the witnesses were able
to identify the object and as with the Longs are completely
mystified by the event.

Investigation
On the evening of Friday 28th May 1993, I travelled to

Axminster to meet and interview KEG ——————- I was
accompanied by my wife BEMieaews who fortunately takes an
interest in my chosen hobby and has, as I call it ‘a nose for
things'! On our arrival at their home, EEC OMM————— made us
most welcome and very soon we were engaged in some very serious
but relaxed conversation. BRtegead spent some time in the army
and had served with the Life Guards, so because of my military

background we had something in common.

/4

- (4)

At ‘the present time BERMAN is self employed as a Refrigeration
Engineer, whilst Buea is busy at home looking after their
baby son.

Once we had gone through the details of their sightings, BEQIeM
took me out into the back garden to view his outlook from
there. The rear of the property faces south and is virtually
unobstructed to the south and south west. His easterly and
south easterly view is somewhat restricted by adjacent
buildings, but he still has a good view of the sky from approx
20 degrees upwards.

Geet explained how he noticed the objects approach and how
amazed he was to see it stop in mid air. He described the
stopping movement as extraordinary and couldn't see how an
aircraft or anything else for that matter could perform this
feat. He then described the objects circular aerobatics and its
movement between them. Se@uiestimate of the objects height
(between 10.000' and 20,000') is I believe fairly conservative
and having discussed this with him and assessing the sightings,
I believe its height was in excess of 20,000' and probably
nearer 30,000', but this is pure speculation on my part! Even
if the object was at 20,000', then from the size of the object
given Pei section 4 i.e. a new five pence piece, then the object
must have been of considerable size. Beeuomee who only saw the
objects second and third circular movements and its departure,
agrees with Bimemesestimates viz - height. size etc and like
him is completely baffled by its presence and flying abilities.
are, I believe, quite truthful in their
report and can see no reason why they should report an
occurrence of this kind unless it happened! It's my belief that
this event did take place, and in the way described, and that
their sighting may well have connections with the second report
from Packsaddle, Cornwall.

{5
(5)
This sighting occurred on the 6th July 1993 and it has many
similarities to the Axminster sighting. My investigation into
this sighting has been mainly by telephone, but with the added
advantage of seeing REQ and his family being interviewed
on local TV concerning this event.
Sectidie a fisherman, operating his own business from Penryn and
holds a skippers licence.
As with Sede) sighting, Bee hed gone outside into his
back garden for a smoke. It was 11.18pm and being a clear night
was taking in the late evening sky. Suddenly he became
aware of a brightly lit object appearing over the top of his
house and moving very fast. It was on a S.E. course and at a
great height. He estimated the distance of the object from him
to be a ‘few miles' and at an elevation of 60 degrees to the
horizon.
Whilst watching the objects progress, it suddenly came to a
stop and remained stationary for a few seconds. It then moved
forward in a zig-zag movement, for approx 20 seconds and then
descended very fast towards the S.E. almost as if it was
heading towards the moon!
During its forward movement, Bifmwas) able to call out to his
son Baie) and his friend who both witnessed the objects
departure.
From my conversations with Bipan) the TV report, I see no
reason to disbelieve this sighting and do think the report
given is accurate. Beectimates that he had the object in view
for some two minutes, sufficient time, I think for him to have
fully appreciated and noted most if not all of the details.
Both Rime son and his friend have verified the sighting and
all are equally puzzled by what they saw.

/6

(6)
Conclusions
From the descriptions of these sightings, it is fairly obvious
that what was seen on these two nights was not a conventional
aircraft or helicopter. No aircraft to my knowledge, and this
includes the Harrier Jump Jet, can suddenly stop in mid air
from this sort of speed. In addition, I don't believe that an
aircraft could perform the type of aerobatics described by the
witnesses. Because of the sudden stopping of these ‘crafts’ it
is also reasonable to assume they were not Satellites.
Fireballs, Bolides or any other known natural phenomenon.
This being the case - what were they?
They can of course be simply classified as ‘Unidentified Flying
Objects’ - as this they surely were, but into what category do
we place them.
Are they ‘alien space ships' piloted by ‘alien beings' or some
advanced form of air/space craft developed here on earth?
Maybe the answer lies somewhere between the two!
During the last couple of years or so, there has been much
speculation about the existence of captured ‘Flying Saucers'.
this obviously relates to the so called Roswell incident of
1948. If, and I only say if, the USAF is in possession of some
alien space craft, then it is quite possible that they have
been subjected to some very intensive back engineering and as a
result are now being flown by them. It's also possible, I
suppose, that these craft could be piloted by their original
crews - but I think that's best left well alone for the time
being.
The reason for this speculation is of course the stories
emanating from America and the now infamous Area 51 scenario. I
recently obtained a tape recording, made in the USA from a
program called 'For the People’. On this tape some well
intended and apparently well informed person, is heard
disclosing recent events and some film footage taken of these
events over Area 51 (Groomlake) which depicts brightly coloured

/7

(7)
objects rising from behind some mountains and traversing the
sky at great speed. They are then seen to hover and move
forward in a zig-zag motion before moving away at great speed.
If these reports are genuine, then this must open up a debate
as to whether these objects are being flown outside the US and
if so, are they being seen in the skies over Britain.
Both the objects mentioned in this report were seen to approach
from the N.W.,which may indicate that they came from across the
Atlantic - travelled north of Ireland before crossing the S.W.
of England.
As stated earlier this is only speculation, but without further
evidence as to the objects origins, make-up or mode of
operation, there is little else to go on.
There is no doubt in my mind that the reports given by
ECO ace genuine, and that what they witnessed
cannot be explained by any known natural phenomenon.
The ariel performance by these objects is to say the least
quite remarkable, and if they do turn out to be some form of
advance air/space craft made on this earth, then perhaps the
billions of dollars being spent on the so called ‘Black Budget'

May some day be justified!

UFO |
riddle
solved

THE mystery surround-
ing reported UFO sight-
ings in Carmarthenshire
and East Dinefwr may
have been solved.

Strange lights had
been seen in the skies
on the Swansea to Car--
marthen road in recent
weeks and two local
women claimed that the
lighta followed them all
the way from Swansea
to Llanddarog.

But the Gwernllwyn
Country Club at Cross
Hands has admitted that
their laser show could
have been seen as far
away as Swansea.

“The beams go up to
the sky and it seems
that they have been
spotted on a_ clear
night,”.said a spokesper-
son for the popular
nightspot.

And although the the-
ory has ita sceptics who
prefer to believe in visi-
tations from another
world, the fact that most
reported sightings have
been on the same
stretch of road at night
lends weight to the laser
show being the cause of
the mystery lights.

But a spokesman for
the Psychic Research
Group said: “There has
been a huge upsurge of
reported sightings in the
West Country and South
Wales in recent wecks.”

Most of these have
involved lights although
one reported sighting by
a woman in Pendine
was a cigar-shaped fun-
nel of smoke that rose
straight up from the
ground and veered off to
the right.

Other possible expla-
nations that have also
been put forward include
night flights by the
Dyfed-Powys police heli-
copter and the RAF
Hercules from Swanaea
Airport.

‘Mystery
‘UFO’ is
sighted

WAS It a plane? Was it a
helicopter? No — It was a
UFO, according te Gwent
folk who say they saw

something mysterious
flying above them this
week. :

The Argus has learned
of two alghtings, one by
four teenagers at the
Tesco roundabout at
Nash, Newport, and the
other at Rogorstonc.

The UFO described In
the first sighting was
eTey/white and milk.
bottle shaped, with no
bright lighta,

The second sighting
was of a very circular
object with bright Ughia
all round it.

Newport police have
not received any reports
ot UFOs.

3/ TF 199%

“Why aliens don’t
make contact

JOHN Samson, of 12 Red-
wood Drive, Haywards
Heath, writes:

IN response to your invita-
tion for comment on the
mysterious lights in the sky,
ao fact or two might be
helpful.

Whether or not these local

-sightings are genuine, we
should remember that obser-
vations or unexplained aerial
phenomena are a matter of
well-documented historical
record since pre-biblical
times.

However, it was not until
2pm on June 24, 1947, that
an event really caught the
public imagination of the
20th Century.

A Mr Kenneth Arnold,
piloting his own private aero-
plane over Washington
State, USA, saw nine objecta
fiying in a V-shaped for-
mation, which he assumed
were military jet-planea.

But he then noticed that in
appearance they were no-
thing like aircraft, and subse-
quently described them os
resembling saucers skipping
over water — and a new
phrase was born in the
English language: “flying

saucers”.

They differed from any-
thing known to contem-
porary aviation, and Mr Ar-
nold estimated their speed as
1656.71 miles per hour.

So, the answer to your
question: ‘Do such things
exist”, has to be ‘Yes’,
especially in view of the accu-
mulated testimony since
1947, — including film-
footage and photographic
evidence.

And the answer to the
other frequent question:
‘Why, if these things are
manned by intelligent be-
ings, are they 60 elusive and
reluctant to make contact
with us?’, is simplicity itself.

If these things are as far
ahead of us morally as they
clearly are technologically,
we must appear to them in
much the same way asa tribe
of warlike cannibals must
have appeared to the early
Western explorers of darkest
Africa.

They obviously believe —
and rightly so — that we are
better studied from a dis-
tance, and any close encoun-
ters kept to a discrect mi-

nimum. —_

another out of this world
talk at the Cinque Ports

author of Above Fop
Secret, Alien Liaison
and Alien Update will be
discussing and
answering questions on
the great mystery of
whether there is other
life in the universe.

Mr Good, who is also
a erotessional violinist,
offers evidence via
official) world govern-
ment documents, some
classified, others rel-
eased under the Free-
dom of Information Act,
key witnesses and photo-
graphs.

He believes there is a
worldwide conspiracy by
government agencies to
keep information from
the public and claims the
Americans have recov-
ered alicn bodies and
are currently testing a
number of alien vehicles.

His 8pm talk at the
Bohemia Road hotel on
Friday ts the first in a
new series of The Pem-
berton Lectures.

Tickets are £4.85 for
adults and £4 for OAPS
and students. ,Ring

People tend to scoff at
such ‘sightings’, which
is probably why the wit-
nesses won't talk about
their experiences. Mine
happened with two

other people. it was _

early evening, just gett-
ing dark, and we were
feeding ponies,
approaching the field,
buckets on arms, and
usually the two ponies
would be hanging over
the gate watching us.
This particular night
they were galloping
around, panicking, as if
something had really
‘spooked’ them. We
got to the gate, when a
‘humming’ noise from
above attracted our at-
tention.

Above the field, and
literally ‘hanging’, was
a huge object, the best
description of which is a
huge catherine wheel,
revolving fairly slowly,
with bands of colour
radiating outwards,
mauve, yellow and
orange. It didn’t seem
to be far above - not
nearly as high as a plane
for instance. 11 ap-
peared to be hovering,
and - the atmosphere
was ‘strange’ -] can ela-
borate on that no more,
it was just ‘strange’ -
and the ponies sensed it
too.

It was probably only
seconds - but it seemed
like minutes it spun
above the field, then,
suddenly, it speeded
up, revolving now al a
ternific speed - and sped
away.

We were absolutely
dumbfounded. Had it
have been just one per-
son, they would have
been judged as ‘seeing
things. But three
people together all wit-
nessing the same must
be taken seriously.

When we had reco-
vered sufficiently, 1!
reported what we had
seen, and we were
closely questioned by
the authoritics, who
didn't scoff, and took
statements from us in-
dividually.

No explanation of
what we had seen was
ever given.

So, yes, 1 shall cer-
tainly enjoy meeting
people with strange

totally unexpectedly.
“Thought you were as-
leep, we've been out

white aren't you very
well?” | collapsed with
laughter - and relief.
That's my boy!

after yourselfs.

We have been advised by our local Police Station of your address, as

between 8.45 and 9.00 PM, in Weston Super Mare at the Bleadon Level,
Salt water on the River Axe, a member of our local Sporting Club and
myself were walking away from the sea wall towards our vehicles, when a
ball of light the size of a football (a dim not a bright light) came
straight towards us, with a rush of air traveling at a great speed, not
more than 30 yards high, just to our left, it went straight towards the
sea wall and without seeming to stop, just came straight back towards us
again from the other way, and disappeared across the level with the same
rush of speed, another member of our club was following behind and when
we said to him, ‘you will never believe what we have just seen' he
described exactly the same experience.

“Stroud is a UFO hot
spot’ says top expert—

STROUD is a major area for sightings of flying
saucers, a UFO expert declared this week, writes
Adrian Blyth.

UFO investigator Joanne Wilding, who is part
of a top UFO investigation team based In Chel-
tenham, said the area had recently attracted a
great deal of strange sightings, the latest of
which was reported this week.

“We get reports of a lot of sightings in and
around the Stroud areg — it seems to be a bit ofa
hot spot,’ she told the News and Journal.

Only this week Avening resident Mrs June
Burge told how she saw a bright orange UFO
flash across the sky, Mrs Burge, of Rectory
Lane, Avening, said she saw the object while
travelling through Kingscote with two
colleagues.

‘An umbrella-shaped object shot across the
sky then disappeared within three seconds. It
was eerie,’ she said. Mrs Burge, who works asa
textile spinner at Cam Mills, had just finished a
nightshift and saw the mystery object early last
Wednesday morning.

‘IT was shaking like a leaf for hours
afterwards. ] didn’t use to believe in UFO's but
now I’ve changed my mind,’ she added. .

And the News and fournal had its own
extraterrestrial experience when sub-editor
Jeremy Griffin spotted something strange while
driving home down the Ad6 Bath Road, He told
colleagues the next day: ‘It was midnight but
half the sky was glowing red, with a strange
orange object moving in the middle of it.”

10 SEP 1993

New sighting

of a UFO

THE skies over Andover are taking ona
technicolour hue as a second sighting of a brightly
coloured .UFO was reported to the Andover
Advertiser. .

Derek Vine was just about to leave his Admirals Way
house at 5.15 am one day last week when he saw a
brightly coloured ball of green light shoot across the top
of Ladies Walk and then vanish.

The earlier UFO sighting was of a huge, silver
coloured object over Goodworth Clatford.

Describing his experience, Derek Vine said: “There
were high flying aircraft around at the same time but it
wasn’t anything to do with them. At firat I thought it
could have been connected with military manoeuvres
at Middle Wallop Army Air Corps, then I saw the report
in the Andover Advertiser about a possible UFO at
Goodworth Clatford and now Iam wondering."

He has reported the facts to Malcolm Terry, organiser
of an unexplained phenomena group at Enham
J Alamein.-

The Times, London q
3 SEP 1995 /

Strange But True? -
ITV, 9.30pm
Did you know that Todmorden, on the edge of the
Pennines, is the Ufo capital of the British Isles? If not,
Michael Aspel is on hand to tell you all about it. Of all
the sightings of flying saucers and the like, one in ten
occurs in Todmorden. Yet the folks who live there seem
balanced enough, not easily taken in. One of the main
witnesses to strange shapes in the sky is actually a
former policeman. Still, as Aspel admits, you either
believe it or you do not. His second excursion into the
ranormal features a mother of two from
orthamptonshire. She believes that she is the
reincarnation of an Irish woman who died in the
1930s. In its way the story is even more unlikely than
the unexplained goings-on in Todmorden.

TWO Carmarthen women
were left terrified after
their car was followed for
thirty miles by a strange
circle of light that they
claim was a UFO.

Diane Jones and Lorraine
Fox were travelling with their
children from the Mumbles to
Carmarthen in pitch darkness
when they spotted a strange
white light focusing on their
vehicle that seemed to be
“buzzing” them.

“There was a circle of little
lights fixed on the car and
they seemed to be following
it,” said Lorraine of Glynderi.

“I thought nothing of it but
when it was atill there after
we joined the dual carriage
way, it set me thinking.” .

Not only did the lights seem
to follow the car at high speed
but the journey - that would
normally take at least 45 min-
utes - was cut down to a
mere 25, almost as if the
friends went through a Dyfed
version of the Bermuda Trian-

gle.

“The light was by my side
all the time and we couldn't
lose it or get out of the
beams,” Lorraine continued.

“The children got a_ bit
frightened because it came into
the car and focused on them
and lit up the inside.

“It was above us, at the
side of us, always panning us.”

Lorraine Fox and her children Neville, aged 15, and Sarah, 8.

The “UFO” also affected the
car engine which shuddered,
started to whine and felt like
it was going to cut out.

Lorraine's son Neville devel-
oped burn marks around his
wrist where his watch had
been and her daughter Sarah
had a rash around her neck.

Both Diane and ‘Lorraine
have suffered earache- since
the event. me

A local paranormal expert,
Carmarthen resident “Peter
Grosvenor, believes-that the
lights may well have been an
unidentified flying object. .

“The interesting point about

ee

the case is that the lights foi-
lowed the lady’s car for a
number of miles,” he aaid.

“There are over 38,000 sight-
ings of what people may think
are UFOs each year but peo-
ple can be sceptical about this
type of thing and that's a
shame.”

Businessman
fears ridicule

over UFO report

A RESPECTED Bristol business-
man had the shock of his life when
what seemed to be a massive UFO
hovered above him as he travelled
to work.

The middle-aged man, who did
not wish to be named for fear of
ridicule from business colleagues,
is convinced Uhal the cross-shaped
object was not from this planct.

He was driving to work in the
city at five in the morning when he
noticed a light in the sky. He
ignored it until the object came
closer, when he stopped the car,
the rotating shape then travelled
tight over his head, at about 100
feet above him.

It was spinning on its own axle,

until it banked sharply on a 45
degree angle and sped away — trav-
elling towards the Happy Landings
pub in Wells Road.

Strange radio signals.

Winterson, who has himself

sightings without ridicule” as’
an active UFO search organis-°
ation. ‘

respond to calls sent to the
group's Hotline.

Muhammad Ali has had a
number of sightings and
says that in the early morn-
Ing you can see UFOs play-
Ing tag between the stars.
“rm convinced they are of
tremendous importance,’ he,
says.

Independent UFO Network
organiser Philip Mantle has
been an amateur UFO in-
vestigator for many years.
He stresses: ‘‘We are
amateu every sense of
the word," and adds that the
organisation Is! a healthy
mixture of those who do
belleve in UFOs, those who
are sceptical and those who
subscribe to a variety of

. theories, L

“| am the proverbial fence-
sitter,”’ says Mantle.

“Lam convinced something
Is happening, but the nature
and origin of it still eludes us.
There are many interesting
possibilities.” .
“Some believe that UFOs are
nothing more than rare,
natural phenomena created
by the earth itself,” says
Mantle. Others think their
experiences: are spiritual.

while some believe there is a
psychological explanation
for the seemingly inex-
plicable.

Mantle says people should
be left to make their own
minds up about what they
believe or don't believe after
looking at all the available
evidence.

His own sighting of a UFO
was on July 24, 1984. He was.
driving home from work
when he saw what he des-
cribes as “two pearly white
luminous spheres” over the
Yorkshire town of Middleton.
“They disappeared before
my very eyes,” he says.
Many other people trom
miles around also reporied
seeing the same thing. No
explanation was discovered.
“There Is definitely some
kind of phenomema at
work,’ says Mantle, who
Points out - as do others -
that too many sane, ratlonal,
sensible and ordinary people
from all kinds of back-
grounds have made these
“unusual observations".

It was his curiosity which led
him to become a. UFO in-
vestigator: ‘‘I don’t like
puzzles, | like to be able to
solve them. f was Interested
An the paranormal when | was
@ young boy - that was just
curiosity too.

‘h came across the UFO
thing quite by chance - itis a
huge puzzle. if the day
comes that | am satistied that
any one theory fits the bill as.
far as | am concerned then I
am finlshed. It won't even
matter if I’m the only person
who belleves that particular
theory."

TAVISTOCK TIMES

16 SEP 1993

Strange, silent craft sighted

flying across

DID ANYBODY see anything strange
in the sky last weekend?

Drawn outside by a commotion in
the car park at around 12.35 on Sun-
day morning, Ray Cornforth of Spring
Hill Hostel and Flats noticed some
lights in the sky approaching from the
direction of Plymouth Road.

As the lights got nearer, they turned
almost 90 degrees and Mr Cornforth
‘saw two rows of cabin windows in the
thing’ which he said was ‘the size of a
medium-sized aeroplane but had no
wings and appeared to have two fuse-
tages like a catamaran.” He estimated it
was about 1500-2000 feet up in the sky.

In view for about a minute altogeth-
er, it then went through Tavistock
along the line of the main street and on
towards Okehampton before disap-

“pearing from view.

‘The strange thing was that there
was no sound whatsoever,’ said Mr

the night sky

Cornforth, ‘and there were five or six
people there with me who would testify
that there was no nolse.

‘] asked them what they thought it
was and they said they thought it was
an aeroplane, but there was no noise
and I checked with British Airways -
there was no commercial flight at that
time and there were no military flights
on Sunday morning.’

Asked what he thought it was, Mr
Cornforth said: ‘Well, it was obviously
a UFO because it was an unidentified
flying object, but I don’t know what it
was.

‘] was quite excited to see it actually.
But I’m not looking for publicity,’ he
said. ‘] just want to know if anyone else
saw it’

Did saybody see anything strange im
the sky last weekend?

9 September 1993 ‘

Dear Nick,
Many thanks for your letter of 19 August.

The BA pilot’s sighting took place in the 1960’s! Graham
Sheppard is a good friend and would be pleased to meet you
sometime. As a result of too much media publicity over his
sightings, he was threatened with suspension by BA, but the
threat has now receded, I’m glad to say.

I enclose a couple of reports for your retention. The Fosse
Way report is good, I think. The other one, while indicative
of a genuine UFO, is not so interesting, but you may possibly
be able to correlate it with others in the same area at the

time.

My girlfriend and I would be pleased to have you over one
evening, with your girlfriend. Just let me know when it’s
convenient.

STANDING ORDER E4

REPORTING OF UNIDENTIFIED FLYING OBJECTS. = (E:1:1)

Natlonal Air Traffic Control Service

1.

The Department of Trade and Industry, National Air Traffic Control Services, are required
to advise the Military Aeronautical Information Service of all reports of unidentified
flying objects and/or similar phenomena coming to their notice. To be of the greatest
value, these reports are required in a standard form, giving as much information as
possible. When a member of the general public reports such a sighting to the police, it
may help then to give the required information if they can be prompted by the correct
questions.

Set out below is the format to be used as the basis of any notification to the Department
of Trade and Industry when any sighting is reported to the police:-

(a) Date, time and duration of sighting.

(b}  BDeseription of abject (number of objects, size, shape, colours,
brightness, sound, smell, etc.) :

(c) Exact position observed (geographical location); whether
observer was indoors or outdoors; whether the object was
stationary or moving.

(d) How observed (naked aye, binoculars, other optical davice, stil!
or movie camera).

(e) Direction in which object was: first seen (a landmark may be mire
useful} than a badly estimated bearing).

(f) Angle of sight.
(g) Distance (by reference to a known landmark wherever possible).

(h) Movements (changes ta {a}, (f) and (g) may be of more use than
estimates of course and speed).

(J) Meteorological conditions during observations (moving clouds,
haze, mist, etc.).

(kK) Nearby objects (telephone lines, high voltage lines, reservoir,
Take or dam, swamp or marsh, river, high buildings, tall -
chimneys, steeples, spires, T.¥. or radio masts, generating
plant, factories, pits or other sites with flaodlights or other
night lighting).
{1} Officer to whom reported.
{m) Name and address of informant.
(mn) Any background on the informant that may be volunteered.
{o) Other witnesses.

(p) Oate and time of receipt of report.

(q) Is a reply requested?

TODMORDEN lies in a deep valley
between Yorkshire and Lancashire,
and if we are to believe Strange But
True? (9.30pm, ITV) it's a favourite
tourist spot for visiting aliens. .

Programme for a possible LWT
series combining personal accounts
with dramatic reconstnuctions of
bizarre stories.

The Todmorden tale starts when PC.
Alan Godfrey is sent to investigate a
body found dumped on top of a coal
heap ina railway yard. A liquid
substance was found on the back of
the unfortunate victim's neck, and
forensic scientists couldn't identify it.
Soon afterwards PC Godfrey was
out in his patro! car when he came
face to face with a UFO the size of

a double-decker bus. Perplexed by
_ the presence of an alien charabanc

party, he tried to call for help but

ound neither his personal radio nor

his car radio would respond.
Next thing he knew it's hall an hour
later and he's 100 yards further

down the road. Whether this has
anything to do with the fact that Mr

SPACE INVADERS

3 SEP 199%

Farmworker
sees UFO

UFO buffs believe a
massive space ship has
been sighted over
Goodworth Clatford.

A 60-year-old
farmworker says he saw a
huge disc, about the size of
a house, spinning above
Gypsy Lane.

“I backed my jeep out of
the wood and there it was
in front of me about 80 feet
away, swinging back and
forth,” says the man, who
doesn’t want to be
identified.

“When it swung
towards me I could see the
underside which was like
old silver that hadn’t been
polished,” he added.

“It was a cold morning
and there was a heat haze
coming off the top of it.

“Then it suddenly
totally disappeared.

“I wasn’t frightened at
all, it was just amazing,
T've never seen anything
like it,” he said.

Andover’s own
unexplained phenomena
investigation group
AUPIN UK believes the
sighting to be genuine
elimpse ofan alien
spaceship. .

AUPIN president,
Malcom Terry from
Enham. says the Clatfords
have long been associated
with strange happenings.

“Two years ago 4 large
orange ball was seen in
the air above Clatford,” he
says.

This summer, a com
circle was found in a field
nearby the most recent
sighting

Mr Terry says there is
‘most definitely’ a link
between the circle and last
week's strange happening.

AUPIN members are |
now investigating the site
using dowsing rods to see
if there is water nearby —
according to Mr Terry
spaceships often take up
gallons of water from |'
lakes and underground
Springs.

UG 18

[Failure of Mars
Observer is
blow to US plan
for space probes

it}

Tim Radford
Sctence Editor

TH United States space
agency Nasa last night
began to resign itself to the
humiliation of a satellite lost
in the last hours as it
plunged towards Mars.

The $980 million Mars Ob-
server was the first in a
scries of probes intended to
culminate in a manned Jand-
ing in the next century. It
suddenly disappeared on Sat-
urday as it neared the climax
of u 450 million mile voyage
designed to study the terrain,
the seasons and the weather,
on the Red Planet.

Engineers at the jet propul-'
sion laboratory in Pasadena
worked frantically to regain
contact with the spacecraft,
but last night all attempts
had failed. One theory Is that
the probe exploded as fuel
tanks were being pressur-
ised. Others are that its on-
board clock had broken, its

tenna twisted.

A group of Mars buffs
claimed that a “rogue group”
within the agency had done
it deliberately to conceal evi-
dence that intelligent life
once existed on Mars. Rich-
ard Hoagland ts convinced
that a city which he has
named Cydonia was built by
Martian civilisation.

It is a measure of the de-
spair of the stricken agency
that a spokesman actually
bothered last night to deny
the claim.

The loss of the satellite is
bound to throw new focus on
Nasa’s troubles. It has been

‘loss of direction and expen-
: sive accidents.

President Bush announced
| a manned return to the moon
‘and then a landing on Mars

in 2019. However, he ne-
' giected to put forward any
money.

The history of Martian voy-
aging is mostly disastrous.
The Russians tried for the
Martian moon in 1988, losing
Phobos-1 on the way and
Phobos-2 as it arrived. Alto-
gether the Russians are be-
lieved to have launched 17
unsuccessful missions
towards Mars.

The Mars Observer is not
the only satellite to go miss-
ing this week. The US air
force launched a polar orbit-
ing weather satellite and
then “lost” it on Tuesday.
Unfairly, nobody noticed.

Last night Nasa was star-
ing into the dark, prepared
to believe that the Observer
satellite had flown right past
the planet and on to oblivion.
“It’s terrible, terrible, terri-
ble,” said Arden Albee, the
prolect's chief scientist.

radio overheated, or its an- |

bedevilled by budget crises, -

Daily Telegraph, London

2G REE 1

Calling all aliens

OQ WE believe that

the universe is filled

with super-advanced
civilisations?

As Frank Drake makes
clear in Is Anyone Out
There?, our beliefs in this
matter are irrelevant, Mere
theories will tell us nothing.
The only reasonable course
is to experiment — to search
the cosmos for radio signals
from such civilisations — and
this is what the space agency
NASA is now doing.

They are spending the
comparatively tiny annual
sum of £6 million over the
next 10 years on searching
millions of radio frequencies
coming from the stars hoping
to find one that might be arti-
ficial, How this vast experi-
ment came into being is
Drake's life-story. It was
largely his work. Gnly a
genius at organisation could
have done it. At times he
almost despaired at the out-
bursts of ignorant nonsense
that greeted his proposal. As
one Congressman declared:
“Of course there are flying
saucers and advanced civilisa-
tions in outer space. But we don’t
need to spend millions to find evi-
dence of these rascally creatures.
We need only 75 cents to buy a
tabloid at the local supermarket.
Conclusive evidence of these
crafty critters can be found at
checkout counters from coast to
coast."

I personally do not believe
in alien civilisations — at
least not in our Miiky Way
galaxy. If they existed, their
expeditions would have
arrived here long ago, or we
would observe signs of their
technology among the stars.
The Sun is, after all, only half
the age of the galaxy, giving

; Super advanced aliens ample

| time in which to evolve.
But we see nothing. The
galaxy appears to be lifeless.

Js Anyone Out There? The
Sclentiflc Search for
Extraterrestzlal Intelligence
by Frank Drake
Souvenir, £1650
Allen Update
ed by Timothy Good
Arrow, £499 (pbk)

ADRIAN BERRY

Drake argues that this
means nothing. The galaxy,
he points out, contains some
250,000 million suns, a tenth
of which could be habitable
and a tenth of these in turn
inhabited. In about 10 years
we may know who is right.

Only in one instance is the
professor's memory faulty.
He claims to have invented
the term “pulsar" for fast-
spinning neutron stars. The
actual inventor of this word
was Dr Anthony Michaelis,
former Science Correspon-
dent of this newspaper.

I cannot recommend Alien
Update. It is a series of
essays alleging UFO visits by
people who appear to be
cranks.

The mystery
warnings in
crop circles

PRESS and public
packed out Whitchurch
parish hall recently to
hear a fascinating
presentation into the
mysteries of crop circles

fom Colin Andrews, the
Andover man who has
become an international
expert on ‘the phenomena.

Colin told the assembly
that he linked crop circles
with the deteriorating
ecology of the planet
earth.

His studies suggest that
the circles are a ‘natural
warning mechanism’ .to
humans to mend their
destructive ways.

His speech was warmly
received by the audience
who heard that he had
addressed the United
Nations on the same
theme.

Following Colin onto the
rostrum was former
Troggs vocalist Reg
Presley. .

Reg has been interested
in corn circles since 1990
and he showed a number
of intriguing videos of
mysterious objects and
lights over local cro
circles.

TWO children claim they had
aclose encounter when a UFO
swooped out of the sky — in

broad daylight!

And their normally sceptical grandad
Norman Wild is convinced his grand-
children did see something extra-
terrestrial.

Norman was upstairs at his Burnmoor Road
home on Friday when the two kids — cight-
year-old Jennifer and 10-year-old Gareth —
came dashing up to tell him they had seen a
Strange object flying in the sky outside their
window.

He said: “We all ran back downstairs but it
had gone. So to make sure they were telling the
truth I made them draw pictures of what they
had seen. They did the drawings quite separa-
tely but they are absolutely identical.

‘IT know they are not the sort to tell lies or ro-
manticise about things like this.

“I am usually quite sceptical about these
things but I am convinced they saw something
outside.”

Report:

ADAM MOSS
Picture:

RICHARD ROLLON

with their grandad while their
parents work and school holidays
are stil] unfinished, say the UFO
flew past at great speed and gave
off a sort of low buzzing sound.

Disappeared

They claim it wasn’t a bal-
loon, aeroplane or helicopter,
and the whole family cannot
think what it might have been.
They last saw it as it disappeared
behind some trees in the dis-
tance.

The London Weekend Television
programme “‘Strange...But Truc?"
has gone nationwide and will be

screened across the ITV network at

9 30 pm tonight. 7

Originally made as a pilot programme
for a potential TV series, the show is pre-
sented by Michael Aspel and was first scen
in the London area in May.

Several UFO sightings in the Todmorden
area are investigated in the programme
including the Alan Godfrey case and using
interviews and dramatic reconstructions the
aim is to give the public the chance to make
up their own minds.

Mr David Alpin, a former Calderdale jour-

+ nalist, is editor of the programme and said,

that after studying statistics on UFO activity
across the country, he believed that Todmor-
den and the surrounding area, within a ten
mile radius, was the epicentre.

More than ten per cent of all abductions
and sightings have been recorded here, which

‘he said is remarkable for a smal! town, and

By Amanda Harry

there is no evidence to suggest copycat cases
are occurring because several events hap-
pened before Alan Godfrey's experience.

The programme also covers the subject of
reincarnation and uses information from a lib-
rary of the unexplained in the University of
London made up of books collected by psy-
chic investigator Harry Price.

Mr Alpin said that the programme had ini-
lially been madg as a pilot to test viewer reac-
tion and was onty intended for viewing in
London, but a previous programme they
made on a similar basis was commissioned by
ITV and the response to ‘'Strange...Bit
Truc?"’ means it will be screened across the
country.

The programme was filmed in April in
Todmorden at locations where UFOs have
been sighted and actors take the main roles of
Jocal people involved but an interview is
included with Alan Godfrey and other police
officers from two forces that witnessed a
UFO the night of Alan's *‘close encounter’’.

According to Mr Alpin the aim of the pro-
gramme was to be quite different in its
appruach to the whole area of the paranormal.

SOME of the world’s leading UFO
experts will be in West Yorkshire
tater this month for an international
conference.

Quest international — Europe's largest
mdependent UFO research organisation

The event will be held ai the Civic
Theatre in Cookridge Street on Saturday,
September 25, from 10am to 7pm.

The main speakers include Bob
Gechs! former NASA mission
specialist and a world authority on the
space programme's Involvement with
UFOs. .

Others include Anthony Dodd, a former
North Yorkshire police officer whe is now
full-time director of investigations for
Quest International, and Graham
Birdsall, the Leeds-based editor of UFO
magazine.

Mr Dodd was prompted to become a

tull-time UFO expert following “personal
encounters.”

Mr Birdsall, who ‘ives in Temple
Newsam, Leeds, specialises in classified
information and milltary involvement.

Among the items on display will be film
footage, photographs, documents and
audio tape.

Dorset Evening Echo - Weymouth

1 SEP 1993

Keep

eyes to

the skies for
crop circles!

AS the crop cirele formations come to an
end in cereal crops with the onset of the
harvest, can I please take this opportunity
of thanking all your readers on behalf of
my team of researchers and myself.

Please, readers, farmers, landowners
and members of the general public,
remember that this enigma does not
confine itself to cereal crops only, so it’s
still a case of keeping your eyes peeted for
other crop circle formations.

Can I also thank all the readers who
have sent us detailed information on the
linked part of the enigma, the lights in the
sky and at ground level.

We are building up a database on the
sightings that cannot be explained away
as flares, aircraft or helicopters and are
most definitely increasing in numbers,
not only in Dorset but world-wide from
the reports I have received.

So once again J am asking your readers
to keep their “eyes to the skies" and
report any sightings to our hotline
telephone numbers: (0202) 723127; (0305)
413476 or (0305) 267292 whereupon a form
designed for the purpose will be sent to
them for completion. We will keep their
name and address in confidence If they
request so.

Dorchester is lucky this year to be
hosting an International conference on
Crop Circle Formations and associated
phenomena at the Corn Exchange on the
weckend of October 2-3, and any person
wishing to attend is advised to book their
Uicket now from: The Cerealogist, 20, Paul
Street, Frome, Somerset BAL] 1DX.

Any readers wishing to become
members of the Centre for Crop Circle

"Studies and to receive a quarterly journal

plus updated information on what's
happening in the enigma both in this
country and world-wide should write,

* enclosing an S.A.E. to my public relations

officer, Mr Richard Peacocke, 92,
Poundbury Crescent, Dorchester DT1
2NX.

The conference will be hosted by the
Earl of Haddington and a long list of
national and {international speakers.
People will have a chance to put questions
to a panel of experts.

Can I please inform all your readers
that as from September 3 our address will
be as follows: David and Virginia
Kingston, C.C.C.8. Convenor for Dorset, !,
Pemberton Close, Weymouth DT3 6QY.
Telephone’ (6305) 813476.

DAVIL KINGSTON,

County Convenor,

Centre for Crop Circle Studies,

17, Great Western Road,

Dorchester. —

SPARKS NEW

UFO CLAIMS

A NEW witness has come
forward to substantiate
‘claims of sightings in
Calderdale of aliens and
unidentifield Nying objects,

Thirty-two-year-old Mr
Paul Spence has spoken out
about his own frightening
encounter of a third kind
after hearing about those of
former Todmorden
policeman Mr Alan God-

frey. ---— .
Several sightings of UFOs

in Todmorden were
featured in a TY pro-
gramme last night — called
“Strange But True”, intro-
duced by Michael Aspel.

Twelve years ago Mr
Godfrey, then serving in the
police force at Todmorden,
claimed he saw an alien spa-
ceship while on duty in
Burnley Road, Todmorden.

Several months earlier he
had been sent to investigate
a body mysteriously
dumped on top of a coal
heap In Todmorden Rail-
way Station yard. A strange
substance, which could not
be identified by forensic ex-
perts, was found on th
dead man’s neck. .

In last night’s pro-
gramme West Yorkshire
coroner Mr James Turnbull
admitted he was still baffled
by that death.

Mr Godfrey also repeated
how he got out of his patrol
car and came face to face
with a UFO, the size of a
doubie deck bus.

As he tried to cal for help
his car and personal radio
failed te work and the next
thing he knew it was an
hour later when he was 100
yards further down the
road.

Now Mr Spence, for-
merly of Calderdale but
now living in Allerton,
Bradford, has told the
‘Evening Courier’? that he
had decided to speak out
after hearing about some of
Mr Godfrey's encounters.

“T had a terrifying expe-
rience while cycling back to
my home in Barkisland late
at night some years ago
when J.saw a green human
form radiating blinding
light on the horizon,

“At first, I saw what
appeared to be a red stop
light of a car or bicycle on
the brow and then, os 1 got
closer, a blinding green
light emerged in the shape
of a human being. I could
not distinguish anything
else but it was emitting a
very powerful light,’" said
Mr Spence, who is self-
employed in computers.

“it had a hand up in a
beckoning manner but F .
turned around and fied,
cycling home another
route,"' he said.

Mr Spence said the inci-
dent happened in 1972 but
he can remember jt as clear
as it was yesterday.

place on the U.F.O,
map scems assurcd
after more shock
sightings of strange
lights.

Local councillor Billy
Buchanan had a rude

27 AUG HIS

TALKING POINT

STRANGE lights suddenly
appear in the sky. Your
windows fly open and
standing before you isa
creature worthy of lead role in
a Steven Spielberg movie.

What would you do? Invite it
in fora cuppa or point out that
the fancy dress party is actually
next door?

Joking apart, visitations from
ET look-alikes are taken very
seriously by a large number of
people from all walks of life.

The nearest most of us have
probably got to a flying saucer is
when someone throws a tantrum
in the kitchen. But there are
countless folk who claim they
have spotted UFOs over the years
~ and even those who believe they
have been abducted by beings not
of this world.

Those who say they have seen
bizarre objects in the skies
certainly do not fall into the
nutcase category.

Pop star David Bowie used to
edit a UFO magazine as a teenager
and says he often saw battalions of
alien craft: ‘The came over so

: regularly that we used to time
them.”

By Cathy Gordon

Star Trek star Williams Shatner
saw an object glistening in the
heavens in the Sixties: “I don't

| know if it was a flying saucer, but
I'd often joked to myself that if1
were a little green man and
wanted publicity ['d make contact
with Captain Kirk."

And former boxer Muhammad
Ali has had a number of sightings
and says that inthe early morning
you can see UFOs playing tag

tween the stars. ‘I’m convinced
they are of tremendous
importance,” he says.

Independent UFO network
organiser Philip Mantle has been
an amateur UFO investigator for

, Many years.

He stresses: ‘We are amateurs in
every sense of the world”, and
adds that the organisation is a
healthy mixture of those who do
believe in UFOs, those who are
sceptical and those who subscribe
to a vartety of theories.

“Tam the proverbia! fence-
sitter,” says Mantle. ‘Lam
convinced something is

| happening, but the nature and

origin of it still eludes us. There

are many interesting possibilities.
Ninety-five per cent of reports

| of so-called unidentified objects

vik

OA ‘flying saucer’ — or ‘Venusian scout craft’ — allegedly photographed in Califania in 1952.

are in fact identified at the end of
the day.

“Some believe that UFO' are
nothing more than rare, natural
phenomena created by the earth
itself,” says Mantle. Others think
their experiences are spiritual,
while some believe there is a

psychological explanation for the
Seemingly inexplicable.

Mantle’s His own sighting of a
UFO was on July 24, 1984. He was
driving home from work when he
saw what he describes as “two
pearly white luminous spheres”

over the Yorkshire town of
Middleton.

“They disappeared before my
very eyes,” he says.

Many other peuple from miles
around also reported sceing the

same thing. No explanation was.

discovered.

“There is definitely some kind
of phenomema at work,” says
Mantle, who points out - as do
others -that too many sane,
rational, sensible and ordinary
peo le from all kinds of

ackgrounds have made these
‘unusual observations."

It was his curiosity which le
him to become a UFO
investigator: “I don’t like puzz
like to be able to solve them.

“T came across the UFO thir
quite by chance — it is a huge
puzzle, If the day comes that I
satisfied that any one theory f:
the bill as far as 1 am oncernec
then I am finished. It won't ev:
matter if I'm the only person +
believes that particular theory

@ For information about th
Independent UFO Network
contact Philip Mantle on
0924/444049.
