‘e
Flying saucers and the mugs game

~? AUG y995

‘Fact’ sometimes proves
stranger than science
fiction, Lizo Mzimba talks
to people who get carried
away by UFOs

from the headiines this summer,

but interest in UFOs never
fades. The seventies had Close En-
counters, the eighties ET and the
nineties have Fire In The Sky — a
“true” story of an American tree
feller, Travis Walton, who was ab-
ducted by extra-terrestrials from an
Arizona forest. Could happen to any-
body.

“The majority of people don't
know that they have been ab-
ducted,” insists Tony Dodd, director
of investigators at the UFO organisa:
tion Quest International. “It only
comes to light in certain circum-
stances.”

According to Tony, many people
have suffered unknowingly since
childhood. “People are first ab-
ducted as chiidren. They're selected
— we don't know why — and im-
planted with something in their
body which acts like a tagging de-
vice; Then they are taken to a facil.”
ity and laid on a table where some
sort of genetic engineering is done to
them. But it’s not a one-off experi-
ence for the abductees.”

Apparently not. For Janice Geor-
giou, the visits are so routine she
claims to be on firstname terms
with her alien friends.

“I don’t believe they are totally
extra-terrestrial,” she says. “I think
there is some kind of genetic connec-
tion between us.”

Despite such familiarity, the
meetings have not been harmless.
Janice, a 44-year-old housewife and -
mother, claims she was abducted
while asleep in her bedroom one
quiet Friday afternoon and has been
left with burn scars after she was ex-
perimented on by two aliens. “One
had in his hand a long straight in-
strument,” she recalls. “It looked
white hot and he started to come to-
wards me. With all my strength 1
managed to move my left arm and
push his arm. But the instrument
touched my stomach and burned it.”

So have you been visited or are
you a visitor? Mike Hutchinson,
who has researched the UFO phe-
nomenon, has a simpler explana-
tion.

“Some of these people are lying.
Others, under so-called hypnotic re-

Cre circles may have shrunk

call, start fantasising.”

Such scepticism discourages
many young people from coming for-
ward with their experiences of UFO
encounters, says Philip Mantle of
the British UFO Research Associa-
tion.

“T interviewed one young man in
Wales who after one visit started
writing poetry. He said to me, ‘What-
ever you do, don't tell my friends.”
We try to protect teenagers from the
stigma attached to thew experi-
ences.”

Of course, where there's a slice
of the unknown offered, there's al-
ways someone willing to give you
odds on it turning up trumps.

“We take regular bets of up to
£1,500," says Graham Sharpe of
William Hil, who are offering 100-to-1
odds_on extra-terrestrial life being
discovered within a year. “It’s been
a particularly popular bet since
Nasa started their 10-year search of
radio waves from space.”

Many punters are pinning their

“hopes on Nasa and think it’s just a

matter of time until proof of alien
life in outer space materialises. But
they may have to wait. According to
a UFO conference report released
this year, sightings are well down.

. And the Independent UFO Network

says out of the 100 reports received
last year, only 10 remain unsolved.

“T believe there’s no smoke with-
out fire,” says Arnold West, a past
chairman of the British UFO Re
search Association. “There’ve been
too many unexplained occurrences
to discount it.”

mix up really.

velop a language to communicate
with aliens. The world asks why?

THERE are no Jittle green
men In the Dover area.

Three huge corn circles;
usually associated with UFOs.
have astonished drivers on the
A256 at Waldershare.

And some intrigued passers-by
have trampled through the angry
landowner's field for a closer
encounter,

Bul now a probe by UFO
experts has exposed the circles as
an earthling fraud.

“Ten percent of cases are
unexplainable, and the work of an
extra-terrestrial force cannot be
ruled out. But this one is pan of
the 90 percent which are man-
made hoaxes,"’ said Mervyn
Newell, coordinator of the East
Kent UFO Research Unit.

The circles are at the corners of
a masked-out (riangle at Mal-
mains Farm, Waldershare. Mr
Newell and EKURU investigator
Michacl Lake believe they are
phoney because:

No circles have similar dia-
meters, they range from 29 to

) circles
riddle solved

They’re just an earthling

fraud, say UFO experts

37ft. Even the triangle boundary
lengths swing from 25 to 27 to
46h.
@Checks using a compass have
revealed no electro-magnetic
energy.
®Com stalks have been snapped
broken. If they had been simply
bent over it would show the field
had been subjected to a myster-
ious source of heat.
‘@Holes in the circle centres are 8
tell-tale sign of a familiar
hoaxer's method. It involves ram-
ming a pole into the ground.
Someone then walks in a spiral
around it, Mattening the corn with
a board as he goes along. .
Mr Lake and Mrs Newell
believe three to four practical
jokers may have completed the
overnight prank in up to three

hours.

Mr Lake said: ‘‘Even seeing
the circles from a distance | had a
feeling they weren't genuine,
Whoever did this had a good try,
ut it didn’t take us long to
expose their fake.""

The hoaxers may have been
able to walk into the field using
extremely narrow ready-made
tacks.

The findings confirm farmer
Richard Neil's insistence that the
circles were man-made,

He had told the Dover Express:
“We have had people pestering
us about it and some have caused
hundreds of pounds wonh of
damage to the field by walking
through it."

Even if there is life on Mars it
hasn't shown itself around here...

DT AUG 185
Exploring new

link

in UFOs mystery

UNEXPLAINED flying
objects and mysterious
phenomena will be under
examination at the next
meeting of ihe Cheltenhain
Psychic Research Group.

Guest speaker at their
September monthly meeting
will be David Sivier - a
former Cheltenham student
with a special interest in
UFOs, and earth mysteries
such as ley lines.

His lecture will attempt ta
prove a link between the two
subjects.

Mr Sivier now lives at
Hartcliffe, in Avon, the site
of a recent wave of UFO
sightings.

He previously studied at
the College of St Paul's and
St Mary's in Cheltenham,

Derek Newman, of the
Psychic Research Group,
said: “The link that David

LYNN NEWS & ADVE

will be looking at is the
relationship between UFO
sightings and things like the
ancient sacred sites around
the country. .

“If there is a link — it
would follow that you are
more likely to see a UFO if
you are on top of
Glastonbury Tor than if you
are in the middle of
downtown Gloucester,”

He said: “The last UFO
sightings we know about in
Gloucestershire were in
December 1992. People in
Parkhurst and Pillowell in
the -Forest-of Dean reported.
Seeing strange objects
shooting beams of light on
to the ground.”

The group meeting will be
al 7pm on Thursday at the
Chester Suite of
Cheltenham's Children’s
Library in Chester Walk.

. Shape on top pass over her house in Chapel Lane.

by young and old

A TEN-year-old girl and an 87-year-old woman have
joined the growing band of people convinced they
have seen UFOs in the skies over West Norfolk. In-
lriguing?y, both young Denise Scoit, of Clenchwarton,
and pensioner Margaret Smith, of Pentney, report
seeing mysterious lights in the sky within a few hours
of a similar sighting earlier this month by a group of
Lynn fishermen.

In Denise's case, the date was Wednesday, Au-
gust 11, and the time was about 8.30pm. She said
she watched a large, silent glowing disc with a bubble

Her mother told the Lynn News: “I didn’t believe her
at first. Then | read about the fishermen in the Lynn
News and — without showing it to Denise — asked her
lo explain in detail what she had seen. Her descrip-
tion was so similar that now | do believe her.”

Shortly after midnight, Mrs Smith, of Church Close,
Pentney, was woken by a bright light in her bedroom
and looked out to see a row of lights ‘slowly gliding
over the roofs of neighbouring houses. “I've never
seen anything like it. No man could create it. It was
the most beautiful, peaceful thing I've ever seen,” she
declared. Another shape then appeared and Mrs
Smith said she watched bath for about 15 minutes. In
the early hours of the same morning, four fishermen
claim to have seen brightly-coloured tights and a
strong white beam above Lynn's South Quay. See
also page 7. a

Times News

. 1998

‘We saw a flyin

saucer’ claim

IF you were an alien out for a spin in
your spaceship where would you go
for scenic views and an Renee

out of this world?
Apparently 12 aoe Burgess Hill

— destination for the intergalactic
day-trippers shown in this photo-
graph.

The blue/silver saucer-shaped
craft was seen and captured on film
above London Road by Daniel Elson
and Jonathan Brown, 25.

They were at work one afternoon
recently when Jonathan saw the
mysterious craft. He called Daniel,
21, outside and both watched
amazed as it flew aver their heads.

Daniel remembers: “We were a
bit gobsmacked. It was just like a
flat saucer shape. It was going the
speed an aircraft would go. It did
not appear to be moving that
quickly. Then it went fast and il won
a heck of a speed.

“I couldn't believe it.

- “You couldn't hear anything. |
reckon it was there for 20 seconds
before it went over the building
behind.”

The UFO disappeared but Daniel
managed to take a photo of it.
“When I got the photos back the

- By Jo Porter

man said: “Have you seen this?”
Still shocked by the experience he

said: “What can you say? I don't

know whether I believe in UFO-

type things but it wasn't like any-

thing I’d seen before.”

Possible explanations for the UFO

are:

A WEATHER BALLOON.

Weatherman Chris Holmes of Char-

lwood Road, Burgess Hill discoun-
ted the theory. “All weather bal-

loons are set off at Gatwick and you -

could not mistake that for a UFO.
The balloon also has 6 very, very
long tail."

But he did offer a meteorological

explanation, that the UFO was a
mock sun. “Sometimes you sce o
brilliant silvery blue disc -- these
are caused by very high cirrus
clouds.”
AN AIRCRAFT. A spokesman al
Gatwick said that Burgess Hill is on
many flightpaths. “In certain
weather conditions they can be mis-
taken for UFOs.”

Daniel Elson points out wire he saw the UFO to collea- Captured on film. . .. the mysterious flying object : —

gue Tim Browne

ASHBOURNE NEWS
TELEGRAPH

19 AWG 1993

UFO — helicopter flew low that night

Further to the long running saga ff the “Big thing
with Lights” over Ashbourne the other Wednesday, I
can add to the helicopter theory.

T was just going to bed when I heard a loud
“paddling” sound overhead so | opened the window and
saw a helicopter flying quite low over the Fishpond areca
in a north to south direction. }

Tt sounded “heavy” if you know what I mean, and it
had red and green lights on it.

I looked at the time and it was 11.45pm.

I commented to a member of my family the following
day that I had never seen a helicopter flying at night —
not so low as well.

The helicopter flew off toward the Bradley Wood
area.

I hope this has eased the mind of the lady who
thought “it” was a UFO. — Joan Lane (Mrs), 17 North
Leys, Ashbourne.

UNUSUAL ARRIAL ACTIVITY — SATURDAY 28 AUGUST

1. Addressees may wish to be aware that one of the most spectacular
displays of lasers, searchlights and fireworks ever put on in this country is
due to take place on Saturday 28 August.

2. This activity is associated with a concert by Jean Michel Jarre, at
Vembley Stadium.

3. Addressees may vish to bear this information in mind when dealing with
any UFO reports that may be received at this time.

Section 40 2Z September 1993
eres ection 40

Although I do not think we have corresponded recently you may have come
across my name in association with UFOs.I amin fact Seema
Section 40 with BUFORA (British UFO Research Association).

I have wanted for some time to thank you for the time and trouble that you
have taken in the past two years assisting serious UFO investigators in
this country,notably those who are part of the National Investigations
Committee at BUFORA.I know they appreciate what you are doing under the
limited time and resources on a subject of minor concern to your
department, I am equally grateful for the advice you give witnesses to
contact BUFORA. We have been able to help several puzzied folk this way.

As I think you know, my approach to UFOs is that there is not (as popularly
assumed) a government cover-up to hide knowledge and that you know as well
as we do that 95% of ail reported sightings have a mundane solution if you
apply the effort to find it (eg the recent Bristol sightings which created
so much fuss but which were mainly just venus rising>.Of the remaining 5% I
am satisfied that most, possibly all, but perhaps also not quite all, relate
to what I term UAP (unidentified atmospheric phenomena) - these being
natural anomalies that science has not yet fully caught up with, There are
some intriguing areas left to mull over,notably the alien contact
claims, but these appear to be as relevant to psychology and parapsychology
as they do to any physical reality they may define.

I was intrigued to read in a Contact UK magazine about 12 months ago
(along with bizarre claims about a direct link their team has with the
‘aliens’ shaped like a dentists drill who are making the crop circles!)
that you have supplied them with all of the 'alien contact’ cases on file
at the MoD. They published them - being,in effect,a series of letters. I
recognised most of the cases.A couple were witnesses who had inflicted
themselves upon me after reading one of my books. But I was curious that you
had distributed these letters to Contact, given their views. They do say that
you _had_also released these to BUFORA 4 but as
Section 40, cannot trace them in the files.Nor can I find anyone a
BUFORA wha saw them at the offices.Can you check on this please?

Rumours have circulated around UFOlogy for a time about luncheon
appointments you have had with [Section 40 Rag is not part of BUFORA) and
(who most certainly is,as a key administrator).For a time
there were whispers among UFO buffs,who were mystified as to why such a
thing was happening.I assured them that this was probably just a

consequence of your personal interest in the subject, taking the chance to
talk to Geveiteamse) - but you know how these stories can get out of hand

after a time in circulation. UFOlogy is never short of conspiracy theories.

Sectio mas recently confirmed that the meetings happened, but,so far as he
knows, you did not pass on MoD 'alien contact‘ letters. So this still leaves”
unresolved what happened to them. Your cover letter which Contact publish
clearly refers to copies coming to BUFORA in 1992.

The other point that I wanted to raise was regarding your correspondance
mamas ection 40 over the Rendlesham Forest/Bentwaters saga.As you know, I

have had more than a passing interest in this, writing two books (the more
recent and,I trust, sober of which was published
went on ~ although there was clearly a lot of confabulation involved. One of
the witnesses who reputedly saw aliens (‘Art Wallace' of the original 1983
News of the World articles) is shortly to publish his own book, which may
wall open up old wounds.I have to say from the interviews that I did that
nobody else told me that they recalled aliens.

Anyhow,I was surprised from Beene Op letters that you had not heard the
Halt tape before he gave you a copy. Your letter to him of 14 May 1993 (Ref
D/Sec (AS) 12/3) argues that the tape may be a hoax,giving various
reasons. Having a littie free time for the first in what seems like ages I
thought I would fill in a few gaps on this matter for you. It might heip you
to reappraise your views and to have material to hand to answer future

“queeries. You doubtless still get them and when Art Wallace (ie Larry

Warren's) book appears you may well be inundated from America, where some
may believe the MoD have got spaceships and a few little grey aliens
locked away.Of that,I will say no more, since I have already been sued
personally,as was a British newspaper, for a not inconsiderable sum, when I
countered stories about dead aliens and crashed spaceships, thinking it my
responsibility to the serious side of UFO research to do so...

So best that you be prepared. Although I wiil confine my comments to the

Halt tape I would be pleased to answer any questions that you may have
about this case,or, indeed, any other current UFO activity in this country.
At a focal point of reporting channels over quite a diverse range,I do find
that most cases come through my office ~ so please feel free to contact
me,no strings attached,to ask any questions at any time that might be
useful to you,eg in framing replies that you. may need to give to a
correspondant.

I am absolutely delighted 40 see the cooperation that has thrived in recent
years between your offices and BUFORA.We are both working in the seme erea
and with much the same philasophy I think. The more we can do to help one
ancther, the better.

4

Sincerel

Section 40

The Halt tape

The tape is not a hoax.I em as certain of that as I can be, It has been
analysed by experts in sound recording in the USA and,aside from the
problems of re-recording,it is just what one would expect it to be. Several
of the people on the tape have been identified.I have talked to two of them
myself. They, and others, all confirm that the tape was made and is accurate.

I do not know how many generations on from the original is the copy
that you have,but I have what I believe is at worst a third generation
recording. Fwo were distributed in August 1984 from the USA. They came from
Colone! STE at an air base in Texas.He was a commander at Bentwaters
after the 1980 incidents and decided to release it,he said,as there was no
reason for him not to do so.A covering letter was sent - but not with the
copy that came to me.At the time I was in hospital having my gall bladder
removed,so I never chased it up;although I have seen the letter,It is a
private one fpemsection #1 suspect this was his personal decision,not a USAF
official act.He was under no obligation to withold data and felt the facts
would be illuminated by forwarding the tape.

ISection| ie not present when the incidents occurred but does say he
accepts the credibility of the men who described it.

‘ftade the recording we all have by playing the original on a
portabie in his quarters and picking up the sound onto a second tape
deck, without any direct interface. Thus extraneous room sounds interrupted
that way.They are not part of the original ,I understand, which was
reputedly destroyed by accident. This was made, hy Halt,on a dictaphone style
tapedeck, keyed on and off - mostly off,as they appear to have had other
distractions later on that morbing. The main purpose was to record the
sample taking and site details as it was dark and difficult to write notes.

_ As for the ‘UFOs’ that intrude into the picture, in my view these often
match the location of the lighthouse.Whilst none of the witnesses I have
talked with buy that solution in aby way,many of the lights refered to on
the tape are not totally inconsistent with it in a number of aspects.

I do not know why Halt did not mention the tape on his memo (or indeed
the photographs that it records being taken and which I know for sure were
taken;even though they have never been released).I can guess (and,I
stress,it is purely a guess).I got the impression that Halt may have been
in the forest at 1 am with a group of men on a private venture,rather than
an official mission;although would equipment (‘eg geiger counters,
lightalls, etc) have to be logged in and out? Of course, there may have been
quite proper channels by which the equipment could have been used in an
unofficial capacity that night.I do not wish to imply anything underhand
took place at all.But it would be odd for an official exercise to take site
measurements, geiger counter readings, photographs, samples etc, in the middie
of the night. Why not go during the day? Unless you also wanted to watch the
sky after work as well, of course - which might have been in the equation.

My feeling is that Halt,like you,was personally interested in the
subject and that,after the first nights events, he went out there on his own
initiative, perhaps reasonably assuming an investigation would follow and
need this data. When the USAF seemed disinterested in what was happening and
the MoD also expressed the standard line of "no defence implications and so
no investigation foilow up is necessary' he may have simply sent the memo
(17 days later note) as a gesture for the files rather than as a definitive
account of material that nobody had shown too much interest in analysing.

Just a thought.I may be completely wrong in all of this, of course.

ER TERROR: Families’ cat followed by UFO in 20-mile night-time ordeal
By Caroline Evans

A UFO terrorised a car full of terrified women and children as
they drove home to Carmarthen earlier this month.

Lorraine Fox, her children Neville and Sarah, and Diane Jones and her son
Nicholas say they were followed for 20 miles by a bright object in the sky.

The circular white light followed them from the outskirts of Swansea as they
made their way home after an evening in the Mumbles.

During their dramatic journey they claim:
@ The light seemed to single out the children, shining directly on to them,
before finally shooting off.
@ The trip, which should have taken well over an hour, was done in under half
an hour and they calculate they must have travelled the crucial 20 miles in ar-
ound two minutes.

@ Despite a badly fitting door the car seemed to travel along without any
sound.

@ After the journey there was a strange smell around the car.

@ The next day the two women and two of the children suffered unexplained
symptoms.

‘The strange disc-like light was first spotted by driver Lorraine as they left
Swansea: “I noticed it round about the Smiths factory although there was a lot
of street lighting. At first I thought it was car lights. It was a circle of light on
the right of the car. It didn't seem to be moving around, but.as the car moved
it seemed to move with us.

“When we got to the motorway it was still there so I said te Diane: ‘Can you
see what I can see’. "It came into the car, lighting up the inside so I thought
somebody had turned the light in the car on and I went to turn it off. It was

- very bright and yet we were not straining our eyes,” she said.

It finally left them at Llandarrog. “It shone on to the children, they said it
seemed to come down on to them,” said Lorraine. When they arrived home,
they discovered they had made the journey in just 26 minutes. This, they say,
is impossible, particularly as they had taken a detour through Swansea.

Lorraine says she was not speeding. She says her C registration Rover could
not do more than 80 mph. The following day they found they had .

BUZZED: Nicholas Jones, Neville Fox, Sarah Fox, Diane Jones, Lorraine Fox at the wheel

unusual symptoms. Both women had earache, Diane had swollen |

eyes, 15-year-old Neville had burn marks under his watch strap and
10-year-old Sarah had a rash on her chest.

Since their experience, the children have been too frightened to
go to bed and the women say they feel they have been suffering '
frorn shock.

‘They would all like to believe that what they saw was part of a
laser light show taking place that night in Crosshands, but, when
® they zeached the area, the lightshow and the light that followed

them from Swansea were different.

Police in Carmarthen said they had a number of reports of lights

Mr Peter Grosvenor, of Carmarthen, who has been investigating
UFO sightings since 1954, said he had heard of the womens’ expert
ence and was interested in their story.

August 22,1993 WALES on SUNDAY

“It seems unusual that the light followed them over such a great”
distance,” he said.

in the sky on the same night, but believe they were caused by the
laser show.

But their faith in some future

sadly not reflected among

AMAZED Wearsiders have
sparked a UFO alert after a number
* of unexplained sightings over
Sunderland. ;
Bright lights were reported in

For only a few days ago, flying
saucer enthusiasts gathered
for a conference in Sheffield
to hear some very bad news.
After several decades rich in
flying saucer sightings, ET
eppears to have gone home,

The Independent UFO Net-
work last year received a
mere 100 calls of eye-witness
first encounters. And 90 of
them turned out to be con-
siderably less than

supernatural.

Plague

Belgium had the same prob
lem. Once the most UFO-
infested nation on the
planet, it was down to a
meagre 60 sightings.

Even the much-loved corn-
circles have dwindled. All in
all, it's bleak news for the
UFO spotters who gather
religiously every year on the
hills of Wiltshire. In sum-
mers past, they could be
confident of at least seeing a
flashing light or two, if not
the full orange and red
iuminated works.

Has there been a galactic
space war? Some terrible
inter-stellar plague even now
bound for our planet Earth?
Or could the solution be
somewhat more mundane?

Boredom may well be playing
@ part. After all, any galactic
visitor would eventually tire
of hovering over Belgium. As
for the UFOs’ other happy
hunting ground in Wessex
.. Perhaps i¢ has simply
become the alien equivalent
of Torremolinos. Our mys-
terious visitors may have
fled to quieter climes.

X)

Predict

uestion of technology. In
© age of the Btealth

Bomber, even. an astonish-
ingly fast apparition, like
the one seen over the skies
of Shetland last December,
can be dismissed as just
another modern marvel from
the Pentagon.

There could be one further

tion, UFO sightings

have always strangely risen

proportion to the volume

of science fiction we are con-
suming on Earth.

Wales and the West Country
suffered a rash of strange
visitations in 1978 and 1979,
the years when Star Wars
and Close Encounters hap-
pened to be the biggest
cinema box office earners.

Bo until Steven Splelberg
comes up with another space
blockbuster, we might safely
predict that our skies will
remain sadly devoid of
cosmic travellers.

believe the amazing dinosaur

the other day...

jer.

BRIGHT flashing lights
which moved along Lynn's
South Quay in the night
sky mesmerised two fisher-
men, who are convinced it
was a UFO.

David Hamer (35} and An-
thony Fenton (36), both of
Lynn, claim they were fishing
off the quay at about |.30am
on Thursday when they were
alerted to the strange red, green
and yellow lights coming
across the river by another fish-
erman — a young-man aged 16 -
(8 — wha swore loudly.

Mr Hamer said: “[t wasn't
moving very fast but was com-
ing in at an angle. We followed
it along the quay for about five
minutes, It was about 200 feet
above.

HOVERED

“I hovered for a split second
all the lights went out, then a
bright white light came on un-
derneath and it shat into the
sky like a bullet.” He claims
that when the bright light
switched on he felt his hands
and face bum and itch, “I was
shocked but more fascinated
than scared,” he said.

“The bright light really
bummed, really hunt your eyes. [
couldn't move, I just froze,”
added Mr Fenton.

Hull Daily Mail

Sightings

of UFOs
on the up

REPORTED UFO sightings in
Humberside are on the increase,
according to local experts.
That’s the view Hull UFO expert Chris
Lawson, who was in Sheffield at the
weekend as part of the British
International Conference into
paranormal experiences.

More than 400 boffins from around the
world attended the two-day conference
to hear of the latest incidents involving
mystery sightings.
Mr Lawson said he had received
“numerous reports" of mystery
sightings in Humberside which he had
investigated. ,

Since a local sighting in May in the
county, Mr Lawson said he had -
received another 12 “genuine” reports |
from witnesses who claim they have
also seen a UFO.

In one incident, a couple saw a UFO
while spending an evening near
Withernsea. Resulting sketches
matched almost identically with similar
sightings.

Mr Lawson claimed one of the most
horrifying trends to surface during the
last 20 years was animal mutilation.

In southern England, about 90 animals
-usually cows or steers -had been
discovered drained of blood with parts
of their bodies missing.

Laser-like cutting had removed eyes,
internal organs and cheek bones on
each carcass. He believed the
mutilations are the result of surgery by
aliens, since parts had been removed
by techniques not seen on earth.

By GEORGE LEOPOLD
Defense News Staff Writer

WASHINGTON — Develop-
ers of unmanned vehicles in-
creasingly are looking at com-
mercial applications ranging
from drug enforcement to waste
cleanup as the U.S. Department
of Defense cuts weapon pro-
curement and struggles to pro-
tect its research and develop-
ment budget.

The search for commercial
applications was the focus of an
annual conference here June 28
sponsored by the Association of
Unmanned Vehicle Systems.

“T will put high priority...
ona family of unmanned recon-
naissance vehicles systems,”
William Perry, deputy secretary
of defense, told the unmanned
vehicle conference.

Perry, however, stressed the
need for diversification into
commercial applications be-
cause of deep cuts in Pentagon
procurement and research and
development. “You are looking
at a market contraction of al-
most two-thirds," he warned.

Despite recent reductions in
the number of unmanned vehi-
cles the Pentagon plans to buy,
including smaller numbers of
the close-range and medium-
range unmanned aerial vehi-
cles, joint program offices over-
seeing development are trying
to cooperate more closely to
speed deployment.

Still, officials heading the of-
fices said they also are working
with civilian agencies to widen
the application of robotics and
UAV technology.

Rear Adm. George Wagner,
director of the UAV Joint Pro-

rone Firms Set Sights on Commercial Sale

SKORSKY MACAAFT PHOTO.

As procurement budgets shrink, the Cypher UAV made

the next decade.

Several types of robots
signed for military applicati:
including ordnance dispo:
runway repair and battlefield
connaissance may find appli.
tions in such areas as cleani
up military bases, according
Andrus Viilu, a Pentagon offic
overseeing tactical programs.

“We're seeing some of tl
interest in dual-use technic
ogies’’ through contacts wi
the departments of Energy a
Transportation, Viilu told tl
conference.

While the Energy Depar
ment already is using teleope
ated robots linked to an eper;
tor by fiber-optic cable t
perform hazardous tasks at it
nuclear facilities, cleanup of it
weapon-production facilitie
will require greater use of rc
bots, said Clyde Ward, an eng:
neer in the Savannah Rive
Technology Center, Aiken, 5.C.

“The trend is to go to remot:
operations to reduce exposure
to radiation,”’ Ward told the un
manned vehicle conference.

Among the requirements for
robots are excavating and re-
trieving buried wastes, decon-
tamination and decommission-
ing of Energy Department
facilities and automating the op-
eration of waste facilities.

The Energy Department has
developed a robot called the
Stored Waste Autonomous
Monitor Inspector to inspect
waste drums at storage sites for
corrosion on a weekly basis.

Elsewhere, the department is
demonstrating a robotic exca-
vator developed by the Army
for bomb disposal at its facility
in Idaho Falls, Idaho.

commercial duties such as drug enforcement.

Despite the shrinking military
market for unmanned systems,
proponents remain optimistic

interdiction or monitoring the
condition of public works such
as dams by the U.S. Army Corps
of Engineers.

Unmanned vehicle propo-
nents also cite weather monitor-

ing and assessing the extent of
natural disasters as potential
applications. “There is a spec-
trum of potential applications
wider than surveillance, recon-
naissance and bomb damage as-

sessment,'’ Wagner said.

The UAV Joint Program Of-
fice reported in its 1993 master
plan that federal, state and local
governments are expected to
increase their use of UAVs over

that civilian applications car
take up the slack. ‘We have the
same requirements for commer-
cial and military applications,”
said Dick Wagaman, president
of the unmanned vehicle group.

By CHARLES MILLER

And MICHAEL WITT

Defense News Correspondents.

LONDON — Britain’s Ministry of Defence
is likely to propose fitting a single warhead to
a number of submarine-launched Trident D5
missiles in lieu of developing a Tactical Air to
Surface Missile (TASM) when the ministry re-
veals its annual white paper.

The white paper, an annual document set-
ting out the Defense Ministry's spending and
strategic plans, is likely to be released this
week, defense sources said. It is expected to
reveal ‘many equipment decisions as the min-

plans to purchase 200 Challenger 2 tanks and
more Sandown-class single-role minehunters
and Type 23 frigates.

The document also is expected to offer a
firm commitment to the Anglo-French frigate
project to develop a new antiair frigate as a
replacement for the NATO frigate for the
1990s program. Called NFR-90, the NATO
program was canceled in 1989. But a Britain
needs to replace 12 type-42 air defense frig-
ates at about the turn of the century.

Brian Lowe, director general of Britain’s

British Industry Braces for Equipment Decisions

One analyst who requested anonymity said
the white paper might announce a cut in the
number of Eurofighter 2000 aircraft to be
purchased for the Royal Air Force and he dis-
counted any commitment to the Future Large
Aircraft program because of the estimated $4
billion it would absorb in research and devel-
opment funding.

Eurofighter 2000 is a simpler, less-expen-
sive derivative of the European Fighter Air-
craft being developed by Britain, Germany,
Spain and Italy. Britain has said it would buy

Defence Manufacturer's Association, said — 200 of the new tack

For one heart-stopping moment,

UFO.

Most of us quickly realise that the
mysterious glowing “spacecraft” is simply
the sun glinting on the wings of a plane.

Yet every year dozens of people remain
convinced there is no other explanation — what
they saw simply had to be a UFO.

in the past 50 years, East Anglia has been
visited by flying cigars, mushrooms, fiery globes
and flashing colours.

The 50s, when the public imagination was
excited by unconquered space, were boom-time
for UFO spotters with scores of reported sight-

Give them a buzz

almost everyone thinks he is seeing a .

ings around Norfolk and Suffolk.

Many people believe all sightings can be put
down to galactic events such as the Perseid
meteor shower, which passed by under cover of
cloud on Wednesday night, or to balloons, air-
craft and tricks of the light.

But Anne de Boisgelin, who investigates UFO.
sightings in Norfolk, thinks it is important not
to ridicule people who believe they have seen
something |

With Roy Wilkinson, she interviews people
for Quest International, an organisation that
investigates UFOs across the world.

“I have seen orange lights and half-moon
shapes which have appeared over the horizon,”
said Ms de Boisgelin, from Waxham.

“Whether it was a spaceship or not we will
never know.”

Philip Mantle, organiser of the Independent
UFO Network conference at Sheffield at the
weekend, still cannot decide if he believes in
alien visitors. |

He was a sceptic until he saw strange lights in
the sky over Wiltshire. He contacted the UFO
Network and discovered many people who had
seen similar lights around the country.

Among them is Janice Georgiou, founder of
the British branch of the Worldwide UFO Wit-
ness Register. ‘

She claims she has been abducted twice by
aliens, seen flying saucers since she was a child
and even have pictures of UFOs on video.

“The group.is so important because a lot of
people who have these sort of experiences have
no one else to tura to,” she said.

it’s life but not

Whether you believe in their existence or not, UFOs have become part of
modern life. Hundreds of sceptics and believers spent the weekend
arguing if men from outer space really exist, as ANGELA HURREN

‘North Star’ size
of a 4-lane highway

Despite the ridicule of some of their

friends, the Preston family remain

convinced — they have seen a buge

spaceship.

They say the 100ft Nying saucer

appeared above their car as they

drove into King’s Lyna one night.

In the car were Marilyn Preston of

Tyan § and ber daughters Kim and
erry.

Kim an explained: “As we drove towards

Lyno my sister pointed out what she

thought was the North Star.

“But it just got bigger and bigger. As

we came to the roundabout near

Campbells it was there above us. It

was as wide as all four lanes of the

road, about 100 feet.”

She said the craft was grey with bright
lights all over it. It hovered over a
building before disappearing leaving
the smell of burning fuel.

“1 had always wanted to see a UFO
but when it happened 1 was terrified
and just wanted it to go away,” said
Kim, who has since had dreams of
being revisited by spacemen.

Anne de Boisgelin, the Waxham-based
UFO investigator who interviewed the
family for her research, said:
“Whether it was a spaceship or not
we will never know but I certainly
believe they saw something”

I was snatched by a UFO...
then I had an alien’s baby

By MURRAY
DAVIES

ON a calm summer
night Karen and her
boyfriend were stroll-
ing along a country
lane in Somerset.

Suddenly they heard a
humming noise and a
Strange object flashed
overhead in a terrific blast
of wind.

Later there was a 30-
minute gap in their evening
that they couldn’t explain.

Weeks afterwards
Karen, 19, found that she
was pregnant — although
she claimed it was
impossible,

She began to suffer
nightmares about having
an ugly baby equipped

-with - incredible intelli- -

gence. It was almost a
relief when, after three
months, she was told she'd
miscarried.

Five years later, in 1984,
she heard the same hum-
ming noise and woke to
find a tall blond creature
with large blue eyes at her
side.

She was floated out of
her bedroom window into

hc LED experts trom allover the world wit

aliens are ki

periments that will enable them to

us — fo create anew race.” =

a UFO. She vaguely re-
calls being medically ex-
amined and knowing that
something “very impor-
tant” had happened.

In another incident
three years later, she
awoke to feel tiny fingers
touching ber own. She is
convinced that a little
child was holding her
hand. .

As her husband
switched on the light
Karen saw a ball of light
float from the room.

UFO researchers now
believe that Karen is just
one of a growing army of
wamen being abducted by
aliens.

Between 1965 and 1975
only 20 cases were re-
ported worldwide. Now

there are about ONE
HUNDRED cach year.

UFO experts say it is
part of a long-term pro-
gramme of genetic engi-
neering, using the Earth as
a laboratory. The aliens
have repeatedly told their
victims they cannot breed
among themselves so they
need to mutate humans
igto their likeness for
inter-breeding.

“Human contacts with
aliens are becoming more
sinister. Earlier victims
were invariably men who
enjoyed the publicity.

Women contacts now
outnumber men by two to
one and most are unwill-
ing to talk about their
experiences. Their very ‘
unwillingness to talk lends

: ef

credibility to their expe-
riences, say believers.

Victims suffer trauma
and repressed memories
similar to rape victims.
Only an estimated one in
10 report their stories,

The first reported ab-
duction took place in
BRAZIL in 1957 when a
farm hand claimed to
have been sexually exam-
ined by aliens. No-one
took his story seriously.
But a few years later, in
NEW HAMPSHIRE,
USA, a woman suffered
the same experience.

ft was the start of a

growing explosion of
reported abductions and
medical experiments.
@ In January last year
Maria was driving in the
HUNGARIAN country-
side when she saw alight
coming towards her.

She woke in the middle
of a snow-covered field.
Her lights and engine had
failed. Her body was cov-
ered in burn-like wounds
with unusual puncture
marks and a strange rash.

The car door had been
welded shut through ex-
traordinary heat.

Maria became pregnant
and ultra-sound examina-
lion showed up the image
of an abnormally deve-

. loped foetus, which later

simply disappeared.

: @ Debbie Tomey from
‘ INDIANA began a life-

lime of strange experi-
ences al the age of seven
when, she claims, she met
an unusual little boy who
“played a trick” which in-
volved a cut to her leg.
This, it is claimed, was
the aliens tagging her, as
we tag animals, so she
could be found later.
When Debbie was in her
teens she says she was
twice taken aboard a UFO.
She felt her stomach be-
ing squeezed. Somethin:
was placed inside her and
she felt a strong suction

, device at work.

Five years later, in 1983,
she believed she was
shown her alien child dur-

ing a further abduction.
The child was about four,
with white hair, large eyes
and an egg-shaped head.
@ In the late Seventies
Susan says she was kid-
napped in the NORTH of
ENGLAND and placed on
a table inside a UFO.

She was examined by
creatures with large eyes,
pale skins and very blond
hair, Afterwards she be-
gan to have strange
dreams and became un-
expectedly pregnant.

A few weeks later she
found blood on her bed
clothes and discovered she
was no longer pregnant.

Two types of aliens -
the Greys and the Nordics
- are said to have been
isiting the Earth for cen-
turies. Our forefathers,
knowing nothing of oth-

er worlds, called one
group evil goblins, the
other wise magicians.

THE GREYS are
dwarfs with white or grey
skins and high, pear-
shaped heads. They have
slit mouths with extteme-
ly large, dark eyes.

These are the evil
gnomes and goblins of
legend.

THE NORDICS are
friendly, almost human
looking. Around six-feet
tall, with beautiful cat-like
eyes and long, very blond
hair, they tend to be kind-
ly observers.

They warn that the
Greys are concerned only
with their own survival.

Operation Magple Investigators at the crop circle which appeared naar Cherhill White Horse

at the weekend.

Circle season

AS experts from Britain and the United
Statea gathered in Bath to discuss the latest
theories surrounding crop circles, a 485 foot
long circle complex appeared overnight at
Cherhill, near Calne.

Last weekend's two-day international con-
ference at the Guildhull, organised by the
Centre for Crop Circle Studies, attracted more
than 150 enthusiasts from all over the coun-
try.

Organiser Ms Leonie Starr said: “We ere
absolutely delighted with the amount of people
who have come.”.

Over the weekend, experts discussed sub-
jects aa diverse as the astrological significance
of crop. circlee, ancient and modern
interrelationshipa and peculiarities such as
UFO sightings end lighta seen near circles.

The circles at Cherhill delighted UFO spot-

gathers pace

ters Foeke Kootje and Connie de Bruyn, from
the Netherlands, and American couple Erik
Beckjord and Joun DeNeve. Both couplee
cluim to have captured a UFO on video
during their visit to Wiltshire.

Mr Kootje and Ms de Bruyn filmed a silent
hovering object near Alton Priors, and the
American couple filmed a similar object near
Marlborough.

Mr Kootje’s recording, the clearer of the
two, shows an object with three lights, one of
them pulsating strongly. Mr Kootje said: “It
was definitely not an aircraft, unless. the
British military has one which flies completely
silently.”

Mr Beckjord and Ms DeNeve run Operation
Magpie — with the aim of filming a crop
puttern actually appearing. So far, they have
been unlucky — but they were exger to
investigate the Cherhill complex.

As if the cars, lorries and other vehicles were not
enough, it transpires that craft from other planets are
even using the road. .

An Unidentified Flying Object was spotted near the
toad last week but there have been so many sightings
over the years that this road has been dubbed a ‘UFO
corridor’.

Indeed, the most convincing piece of film of a UFO
was taken by a driver on the A418 just near

- Cuddington.

Peter Day was driving along the road early one morn-
ing in 1973 when he saw a fast-moving orange ball of
light. He had a cine camera in his cat, which he used
for his job, so he pulled over and managed to catch the
UFO on film for a valuable 23 seconds.

Children in Long Crendon were among other wit-
nesses of the light that disappeared as quickly as it
appeared.

Coincidentally an F111 military aircraft crashed later
that morning some mites further north. That Peter had
captured the aircraft ditching fuel was ruled out. Those
who love mysteries have speculated the aircraft was
chasing the UFO before it crashed.

There seems to be no other explanation of the light.

; Curiously the last frame of the film may hold the vital

{ piece of evidence of this being a paranormal abject,

i The last frame shows the hedge in the foreground
remaining in sharp focus but the trees below the van-
ished UFO as being smeared and out of focus.

. One theory for this inexplicable effect on film is a

.. force field emerging from the UFO as it vanished. The
! force field, moving at the speed of tight, would create ~

ia warping of light rays too brief for the human eye (o-
i notice but captured by the camera.

Guardian - London

14 AUG 1993

Shortage of UFO sightings lands
spotters with ‘bit of a bad time’

Vivek Chaudhary

'HE UFO spotters’ lot is not a

happy one at the moment
Tired of being ridiculed over
fake sitings and having to en-
dure jibes about “little green
men” they are now facing a
severe shortage of UFO sight-

ings.

However, despite the drought
of visiting extra-terrestrials and
flying saucers, hundreds of en-
thusiasts will gather in Shef-
field today for a conference en-
titled UFOs — Fact, Fraud or

Fancy.

“Itis a bit of a bad time at the
moment,” said Philip Mantle,
chairman of the Independent
UFO Network, organisers of the
conference. “‘There has not
been a decent siting in ages.”

According to Mr Mantle they
come in cycles. “The last time
we had a decent series of sitings
was about 10 years ago. This
year there have hardly been
any,” he said.

The conference aims to ex-

pose some well-documented
cases involving UFOs as frauds
and will hear testimonies from
people who lay claim to a close
encounter. Last year the Ind-
pendent UFO Network received
over 100 calls of ‘ible sit-
ings, 90 per cent of which had
non-UFO explanations. ,

“We are still investigating
about 10 cases for which there
is no explanation. Many people
do not bother calling because
they are afraid of being thought
of as a bit strange,” added Mr
Mantle. “There is still a great
deal of stigma attached.”

The most p case in his
experience occul in 1980. In
Yorkshire-a family saw a space-
ship land and three tall men in
boiler-type suits emerge waving
instruments. “I still have not
worked that one out yet."

Despite the UFO shortage, a
few places have still been
receiving visits.

. “Scotland seems to be very
popular. We have had hundreds
of sitings from there over the

years,” said Mr Mantle.

Crop circles:

BRITAIN’S most infamous crop cir-
cle hoaxer recently pleaded with
copy-cats in Wiltshire to stop imme-
diately.

And he thanked farmers on whose
land his circtes had appeared for their
tolerance and sense of humour over
the matter.

Doug Bower, who hit the headlines
in 1991 when he :
claimed to be respon-

| Some visitors came from overseas.
; Americans, Canadians and Germans
| were in abundance. Many vidco-taped
the occasion, or took written notes to
«be transcribed into articles for the
many books and magazines which
now feed the “circle scence”.

thing had become like a religion, with
a hietarchy = of ©
“experts”, People held

sible for starting the

_ crop cizcle phenome-

non 20 years earlier,
was in Marlborough
on recently to tell his

we owned up we wanl-

“ALLL want is the
Wiltshire hoaxers to

wasn’t for them there
would not be any cir-

Mr Bower said that
he had lost control of
the phenomenon, and

A celebrated
pioneer crop
circle hoaxer
speaking in
Marlborough
argues that
things have
now got well
and truly
out of hand

onto their beliefs about
the cause of the circles
and followed them

gregation” that in 1975
he and his fricnd Dave

in a field and fool the

was a UFO,

bar to flatien the corn,
pushing it around on
our hands and knees,”

that “self-proclaimed

Doug Bower's visit to the tiny Naf-
ferton Hall was one of only two talks

place lust night, Wednesday, in

About 200 circle fans, “ufologists”

Frings and intricate pictograms using

Picture: COLIN KEARLEY

UFO adds to circle puzzle

CROP circle watchers are Investigating
links between a mysterious new crop
pattern and Unidentified Flying Objects.

The pattern below Cherhill white horse,
near Calne, consists of a number of
circles, half circles and corridors. It
appeared on Saturday night.
ADutch-American team with a camcorder
and tripod has been tooking at the welrd
pattern which it belleves may be linked to
a UFO incident it clalms to have filmed

earller the same night near Marlborough. -

The enthusiasts are now appealing to
local farmers to allow them to leave
messages In harvested cornfields or
display strings of coloured lights to heip

them communicate with what they beileve
to be an allen force.
Pollce were called to the scene at 7pm on
Sunday when hoaxers were reported to be
making circles, but the “criminals” turned
out to be a team studying the pictogram.

Now Americans Eric Beckjord and Joan
DeNeve have set up Operation Magple at
Weesington Park In Caine and are to
spend the next week continulng the
search for UFOs.

Flying Officer Simon Cree, of RAF
Lyneham, says an exercise just drawing to

. aclose over Sallsbury Plain might explain -

the phenomenon. “it Involved a lot of
Hercules flying at night and making light
configurations,” he says. : :

Mystery

object.

baffles
Fred

A UFO was spotted in
the night -sky above
Eastbourne on Tuesday
last week.

Mr Frederick
Ashman, of Pinewood
Close, who states he
does not believe in allen
beings or life on other
planets, was sitting
smoking in his garden
when he spotted a
glowing .3ft-long object _
in the sky.

He called to his wife to
get his binoculars,
thinking there would be
a perfectly simple ex-
planation for the object,
but upon closer inspec-
tion he became even
more baffled.

‘It was long and thin
and periodically would
shoot star-like lights out
of the back. I was so
amazed, I got the next
door neighbour to have a
look and she said she
had never seen anything
like It before."

He sald the object was
extremely high in the
sky, and after he re-
ported the sighting to
Eastbourne Police,
watched it for almost an
hour and then went to
bed.

He said, ‘When I got
up the next morning I
looked for it again but it
was gone.’

personally, witnessed a bright orange
light flit extremely quickly from one
cloud to the next as I drove down the
Verne Common Road on Portland Island.

The times matched at 9.20 p.m. and

We were led to the site by tne owner. He
asked us how anyone could have crossed
his field, which lies very close to his farm
buildings, without alerting his dogs and
without trampling his corn{as we were
now doing !) We had no answers.

The circle measured just over 42 feet in
diameter with a ring of three foot six
inches or so wide. Separating the two was
aring of pristine corn some eight foot six
inches wide. The corn was swirled in a
clockwise direction in both formations.

circles crossed tow sets of “tram lines” —
the lines used by the farm workers to
allow access to the crops to tractors.
Although a possible method of entry, it
would have entailed an awfully long
detour to reach the circle site.

I give some detail of this formation to
readers of Dorset Evening Echo, as it is
clearly visible to passing motorists on the
Dorchester to Weymouth road as they
come over the ridge into. Weymouth and,
hopefully, a little bit of detail might
dissuade the thoughtless wanderer from

exercise?

Yet another “logical” explana-
tion has been offered for the
UFO recently sighted in the
Green Road area of Ashbourne.

A local aircraft enthusiast heard what
he thought was the sound of rotor blades
that same night and thinks it may well
have come from an RAF Chinook
helicopter — the largest they operate.

‘The aircraft spotter looked out of his
own window and saw various coloured
lights which appeared to be stationary
just west of Greenway in Ashbourne.
Through his binocuiars he then saw

the craft head in an easterly direction
towards Carsington Water.

“Twenty minutes later there was
another sound of rotor blades and a
“second” helicopter seemed to follow an
identical track to the first aircraft.

The spotter thought it may be a
Chinook, as these helicopters have two
large rotors, of about 60 fect diameter
and the fuselage itself is about 50 feet
long. The helicopters are capable of
carrying about 40 troops and equipment
and the spotter suggests there may well
have been a military exercise in this area
that night.

strange beings and lights in the night
sky have intrigued mankind through
the ages.

Natural phenomena, mistaken ident-
ily, alien spacecraft or just plain hoaxes,
they have always been with us.

Once they were seen as gods and
‘angels riding fiery chariots; at the tum
of the century there were cigar-shaped
airships; in the Forties there were ghost
rockets and foo-fighters; and in the era
of sci-fi B movies there were the dome-

. shaped flying saucers that are still with

us today.
Like all good mysteries the flying
objects have stubborly remained un-

: identified, despite the efforts of experts
~ around the world,

Many of those experts are in Sheffield

: this weekend for the Independent UFO

Network's annual international UFO

conference, with a wide variety of
papers written with the public firmly in
mind,

Speakers from the USA, Norway,
Africa and the UK will present papers
on subjects such as abductions in the
USA, UK and Africa, plus case histor-
ies and theoriés on the UFO
phenomenon,

The conference will have its negative
side, too: a Norwegian researcher wilt
be arguing that his research into the
well-known Spitzbergen incident of
1952, when a crashed UFO is alleged to
have been found in the ice, can finally
be dismissed as a hoax.

Theories attempting to explain the
phenomenon will come from Hilary
Evans and Paul Devereux; well-known
UFO writer Jenny Randles will be
focusing on the so-called Cheshire
Window ~ and the latest UFOs cap-
tured on film will be screened by IUN

Reading Evening Post
HW Aug 3

_ Lt]
Whitley
Wood

ESIDENTS were
woken in the night
a mysterious

“swishing” noise.

After several minutes
the noise abruptly ceased,
leaving people wondering
what had been happening.

One man who has lived
in Whitley Wood for 40
years said he had never
heard anything like it and
thought that Martians were
landing.

The man, who lives in
Spencers Road but asked

~ehot to be named, said: “I
was half asleep but when
this noise started it was
like someone breathing
heavily in my room.

“l opened the window
and the noise was so loud
it filled the night sky. 1
couldn't tell what direction
it was coming from.

“I am not a believer in
litle green men from Mars
but ] really did think it
could be a UFO or
something.

“But when I woke the
wife up after the noise
Stopped, she Just thought
T'd been drinking!”

People in surrounding
roads were also woken by
the sound.

Environmental health
and noise control experts
at Reading Borough
Council said they had not
received any complaints.

However, they are as
baffled by the noise as the
people in Whitey. Wood.

A spokeswoman said:
“We would be very
interested to hear from
anyone who was disturbed
or knows what the noise
was.”

What’s up? No one really seems to know

UFO mania really began around
the time of the second world war.
Since then many sightings have
been claimed by East Anglian
people, including:

@ 1990 onwards — the past three
years have been punctuated by
several sightings, but most have
been easily accounted for.
Among sightings were the
strange lights above Norwich in
February 1992, which prompted

"Cuttin

VITA

many calls to the police who
explained they were part of
iNuminations used to celebrate
the reopening of a restaurant.
And in June 1990, UFO
enthusiasts eventually announced
a theory to account for strange
circles which had appeared on a
country lane at Hinghao, ‘near
Wymondham — they believed
they were caused by motorcyclists.
However, July 1990 saw the
arrival of corn circles in a North

Walsham field after nearby
Tesidents spotted mysterious
lights. The circles also appeared
at Tibenham, near Long Stratton.
@ 1980-1990 — The decade
which saw the emergence of comm
circles was overshadowed by the
alleged sighting by American
Servicemen of a space craft io
Rendlesham Forest near
Woodbridge.

The men claimed they saw a
red, sunlike light flying through

the forest, leaving marks on the
ground and trees.

@ 1970-1980 — Norfolk and
Suffolk were visited by lights in
the sky throughout the year.
These lights were variously
reported as blue, green, red and
orange.

There were several sightings at
Lowestoft, and an unnatural blob
of metal was also discovered at
Overstrand beach.

@ 1960-1970 — Pulsating sounds

g a swathe through the corn

were heard in Caister, lights were
seen over Cromer and flying
Saucers were spotted at Lowestoft

Among descriptions were flying
mushrooms, cigars, bell-shaped
craft and fiery crosses.
@ 1950-1960 — UFO fever hit
East Anglia with scores of
Teported sighi
area,

Almost every month brought a
new report of mysterious lights or
objects in the sky.

Bucks Herald ~ Aylesbury.

They are out there...
and they are watching

ERS have contacted The
Bucks Herald backing up Karl
Peterman's UFO sighting reported
in Bishopstone last week.

John and Cynthia Savory, from
Saunderton, claim they too saw &

gigantic Mexican hat-like object

surrounded by bright lights on the

same Friday ‘night earlier this

month. .
Mrs Savory said: “We were on

way to the Rose and Crown in |

Saunderton and saw it out of the
window, It was like a coloured
moon, had uniform lights all the
way round the edge and was mov-
ing very slowly — 1 know it was
not man-made.”

Painter Karl Peterman's sighting
prompted an investigation by

extraterrestial experts, the British
UFO Resource Association.

It forced the association to re-
open its file on the so called ‘space-
ship corridor’ around and along the
road from Aylesbury to Thame, the

Mrs Savory did not take the
sighting seriously at first, because
she thought it was something to do
with the West ‘Wycombe fire dis-
play on that night.

“But then we heard people talk-
ing about the UFO sighting in a
pub and realised we'd seen exactly
the same thing,” she said.

“We watched the thing fly for
about five minutes ‘when we got to
the Rose and Crown. The landlord
John Watson watched with us. But

when we went in to tell people
about it nobody believed us,” she
added.

UFO boffs believe the saucer
hot-spot is something to do with a
combination of cosmic rays, quartz
rock and a.depietion in the ozone
layer.

Together these create nucleur-
like light reactions which can look
like spaceships and are capable of
stalling cars, switching lights off
and bringing down acroplanes, said
director Jenny Randels.

But Mrs Savory, who saw it on
the evening of July 9 disagrees with

the experts.

“] don't know about all that stuff
about quartz the thing was too unl-
form to be a reaction,” she said.

Poltergeists, virgins and falling crabs

MY FRIEND Mike used to spend
his summer holidays by Loch Ness,
looking for the monster. He lived on
a diet of fried spam and got very wet,
but every summer he would be back,
echo-sounder at the ready. Curious
really, because when pushed, he
would admit that he was virtually
convinced the monster did not exist,

But then Mike is a Fortean, one of
a small band of people who continue
the work of Charles Fort (1874-1932),
the American writer who devoted
his life to investigating strange phe-

» nomena with an open mind, On

Monday night, the Forteans gath-
ered at a bar in Notting Hill Gate for
a party to mark the 20th anniversary
of their magazine, the Fortean Times,
published every two months (sales:
20,000 and growing). .

It was an event not to be missed. [
had always secretly fancied myself
as a bit of a Fortean. One of the first
stories I had written as a novice ze-

curter was on a BYM in Belgium. A

Roger Tredre goes to a birthday party where the guests

display encyclopaedic knowledge of some very strange subjects

BYM is Fort-speak for an apparition
of the Blessed Virgin Mary (other
acronyms include SHC — Sponia-
neous Human Combustion — and
the more familiar UFO),

The problem is I have never been
prepared to put in the legwork. For-
teans all possess encyclopaedic
minds, so if you ask them to give you
a brief rundown on, say, poltergeists,
the chances are you'll be with them
until dawa. Last time [ went round
to dinner with Mike, we spent the
entire evening discussing: cannibal-
ism. Just as well we ate vegetarian.
In the crowded bar, the Forteans
drank with gusto. A surprising num-
ber of them wore beards. Mike, who
is now a contributing editor to the
magazine, gave me a guided tour of
the 70 or so guests.

They all had their specialist sub-

jects. John Rowe, a psychology post-

- graduate, was inte crop circles;

Yvonne Greene, a part-time astrol-
ogy lecturer, was interested in UFO
abductions; David Norman, a jewel-
lery dealer, couldn't hear enough
about the Sasquatch, a huge ape-like
creature regularly sighted in North
American backwoods.

Yvonne and David had a tale to
tell me. “We had a joint experi-
ence,” Yvonne said, “in a pub in
Wembley.” :

David saw the look of disbelief on
my face. “It was before we had had a
drink,” he insisted. “The whole pub
went into slow-motion, everyone
was moving around us in strange,
slow, jerky movements. It was very
disturbing.”

Even Bob Rickard, the walking li-
brary who is the co-editor of Fortean

Times, has his fave topic. “I've al-
ways had a soft spot for falling fish,”
he said. They fall out of the sky from
time to time, apparently, all over the
world. It was a remarkably short an-
swer for a Fortean, but then he
opened his mouth again. “And fall-
ing crabs, winkles, worms, insect lar-
vae, snakes, toads...”

There were dissenters in the
room. The editors of Fortean Times
have always been good at writing
about heavy subjects with a light
touch, but some subscribers say the
publication has got too populist for
its own good — shades of the Na-
tional Enquirer or the Sunday Sport.

Fort himself might have had his
doubts, He would certainly not have
approved of Monday night's bash.
When invited to be guest of honour
at the opening meeting of the first
Fortean Society in New York in
1930, he turned it down flat with the
memorable words: “I would soone:
be an elk.”

Case Name: King Alfred Sightings

Witness Reports

a
On the late evening of 3rd August, 1993, a number of independent
witnesses in the vicinity of Stoke St. Gregory, nr. Taunton, Somerset,
observed a self-luminous aerial object.

The object_was apparently first sighted by Silewelmae lof
Section 40 (was driving home from work along
the A361 Taunton to Glastonbury road. Ai 22.40, she turned off of the

A361 and began to proceed along a narrow but well-surfaced lane (known
locally as Cuts Lane} en route to the small hamlet BsiSection 4
Immediately upon entering Cuts Lane, she became aware that a strange
"whirring" sound seemed to be emanating from, she believed, her car.
The car, however, did not display any signs of engine failure and
behaved in an otherwise quite normal manner.

The sky at this time was fairly clear, and to her right she could see

the almost-full moon. Almost immediately, however, she also became
aware of what she at first believed to be an odd cloud formation through
her windscreen and to her left. Julie then observed that the object was
rotating. The object, although she could see no solid form, looked to
her like a wheel with light shining from between the spokes. Somewhat
alarmed, Steg endeavoured to make sense of what she was seeing, and
wondered whether or not one of her car wheels was somehow being
reflected into the sky; she soon realised that this was definitely not

the case.

The lights gave the impression that the object was semi-circular, shaped
something like a tortoise (drawing attached), and was rotating in a
clockwise direction. She estimated its distance to be between 50-75
yards, and it hovered at an elevation of approximately 35° to 45° to the
horizon. The lights had a hazy glow, and were about as bright as car

A
headlights.

About two minutes later, having passed the King Alfred Memorial, Serato
reached another road junction and turned right through Athelney towards
Curload. At this point she lost sight of the object but sensed that it
was behind her. Reaching Stoke St. Gregory some two minutes later, she
caught sight of the object again as she turned into Polkes Field.

Looking north-easterly, she could see that the object had apparently
relocated and_was now in the vicinity of Woodhill Terrace, Stoke St.
Gregory. Section 40 about 1 mile south west of the location of
her initial sighting. Reaching her home at approximately 22.45, Seen
went indoors; frightened by her experience, she did not venture outside
again that evening.

At approximately 22.45, Mr. Bixee)

Stoke St. Gregory, went out of his house to take his dog for a call of
nature. Looking up towards the north-east, he saw a "daisy-shaped",
self-luminous object which was as bright_as the moon (drawing attached).
Calling his MA Section 4 and daughter SERICRBAOM ey watched for
about 20 minutes as the object, “as big as a football at arm's length",
moved slowly backwards and forwards before becoming stationary at a
distance of about 50-100 metres at an elevation of approximately 45° to
the horizon. The object was sharply defined and made no noise. At the

end of twenty minutes, the object vanished abruptly. At this point, two
helicopters were seen and heard in the area by the Biteileam-e)

At about 22.50, Mrs. Ei Stoke St.
Gregory, hearing the sound of her neighbours falking excitedly in their
back garden, went to her bedroom window to see what all the fuss was
about. Looking to the north-east, she observed a self-luminous object
(drawing attached) about 100 metres away, above some farm buildings.
She described what she saw as a circle of white lights, possibly with a
further central light, rotating in an anti-clockwise direction. As the
lights rotated, they moved slowly a short distance to the right, at

first spreading out horizontally then compressing or contracting
together again before disappearing (point A). After a few seconds, the
lights reappeared at their starting point (point B) in their contracted
form and began very slowly repeating a sequence of spreading,
contracting, disappearing at point A, reappearing in contracted form at
point B, spreading, contracting, disappearing at point A, etc. Like the
Section 40 aati that the object made no sound and was at an
elevation of approximately 442 e horizon. Having watched this
display for about 20 RRA Section EW that the object, which was the
size of a football held at arm's length, suddenly vanished.
cannot recall whether there was any other aircraft activity in the area
at or around the time of the sighting.

An elderly neighbour, Mr. Sixewelaag Stoke St.
Gregory, was also alerted to the presence of the object by the sound of

his neighbours talking, having left his house to check that his car was
locked for the night. Although unwilling to complete a sighting form,
PMSection 40 ae kind enough to allow me to interview him informally. He
also reported the object as being slow-moving and self-luminous, and at

an angle of 45° to the horizon. However, he was of the opinion that the

object could have been up to three or four miles distant. He stated

that it reminded him slightly of a World War 2 searchlight reflected

upon a cloud base, although he could see no evidence of any searchlight
beam, and what clouds there were were few and far between, Again, the

object made no sound.

Following the receipt of these reports Section 40 BUFORA A.I. and
DUFORO Chairman, contacted Bane) at_MOD (Air Staff)2a in an attempt
to identify the two helicopters seen by Section 40 | Having promised
to do what he could, Mineptioned that there had been another reported
sighting that evening. In a signal from RAF Chivenor, under the heading
of "Aerial Phenomena", it stated that another couple from Langport,
Somerset, had seen a similar object at the time in question. Beane 25

is now normal practice, very helpfully wrote to the couple, informing
them of our_ongoing investigation, and suggesting that they might like

to contact Biel This they did, and within a few days we
received their report te add to our files.

At 10.45 pm, BEueme Masection 40,

Langport, Somerset, were in the farmyard attending to their animals when
they became aware of a circular series of seif-luminous lights, white
with a tinge of blue and about as bright as car side-lights, slightly to
the north of them at an elevation of 45°-50° to the horizon. Mrs.

ttached) bears a remarkable similarity to the drawings
Pym Section 40 showing a circular formation of
lights with "daisy-petals" radiating from them. For ten minutes, Mr.
and Mem Section dfched as these lights slowly traversed the sky from left
to right, widening, then narrowing before disappearing, then reappearing
in their compressed form at their starting point again. This sequence
was repeated more than_once and bears a strong resemblance to the train
of events described Beasection 40| Again in agreement with the other
reports, the object was described by Mr. and Mrs. feted 2 being the size
of a football held at arm's length. Initially, they had reported a
"drumming" sound that they had associated with the object; this, they
now believe, was the sound of an extractor fan/s working in a nearby
farm building. At the end of, the ten minutes, the object suddenly
vanished. .

Investigation

With all the written reports to hand, SOCOM and I decided that it
was time to interview _the witnesses in person. The first witness we
arranged to meet, was interviewed on the 17th
September at her home. She was quite happy for us to tape our
conversation with her, and, as she told us about the events of the
evening in question, it soon became apparent that she was good witness
material. A young housewife with a lively and intelligent manner,
Sectio| déscription of the sighting did not differ from that which she
had entered in her written report; obviously unwilling to lead the
witness, we allowed Seraa foifelate her experience with the minimum of
interruption, interjecting only when a point needed clarification.

Although obviously unnerved by her sighting, Julie seemed calm and

tational and showed no inclination towards exaggeration. Indeed, she
expressed a worry that what she had seen might have been a "flying
saucer", wanting to believe that it was a mundane phenomenon with a
logical and_easily-understood explanation. It is clear to us that,
whatever Beem saw) she definitely saw something on the night of the 3rd
August.

One area that was discussed was the strange "whirring" sound that had
suddenly started within seconds of her seeing the object. She had
mentioned this to her husband upon her arrival home; the next morning he
examined the car and discovered that there was a broken bracket on the
car's exhaust system. In Sfdfeifeg Strn words, the car was "on its last
legs", and has since been disposed of for scrap. Unfortunately, this
means that we were unable to examine the vehicle - if nothing else, we
might have been able to discover whether or_not a broken exhaust bracket
could have caused the "whirring" noise that Seta had reported, for it

is conceivable that this noise may have been associated with the aerial
object. This, alas, we will never know,

It is obvious from_the_ reports in hand that the object travelled from
the vicinity a Section 40 to the higher ground at BfNemegel Our next
task was to interview —

Like Sizeilela OE i: a young housewife and mother. When

interviewed, she displayed a phlegmatic and down-to-earth attitude that
did much to convince us of her reliability as a witness. Again, we
taped our conversation with her and let her relate the events as she
remembered them with as little interruption as possible. She appears to
have been relatively unaffected by her sighting, merely saying that it
was weird and that she'd never seen anything like it before.

One of the most intriguing aspects of this investigation is the series

of manoeuvres that the object underwent. Although seeing only a
circular series of light sources, feteuel sd@med to be of the opinion that
these were attached to a larger, unseen object. This opens up some
interesting possibilities (see Conclusions).

The are a close-knit, working-class family who are utterly
bewildered by what they have seen. In the words of "It's
the most strangest thing we have ever seen. And very difficult to
explain." Again, I am utterly convinced of their reliability as

witnesses. The only "mileage" that they would seem to require from this
investigation is an eventual explanation for the phenomenon in question.
It is worth pointing out that did not observe the object
for the full twenty minutes: she fled indoors when it appeared to be
moving closer towards them, and admits to having been scared at the
time.

Section 40) _| is a rather pleasant, elderly gentleman in his seventies or
thereabouts. Although unwilling to put pen to paper, he gave me a brief
verbal resume of events from his point of view. Apart from the
discrepancy over distance, his account tallies with those of his
neighbours. He had remarked upon a similarity between the object and
the effect of a searchlight beam: upon leaving, I asked him if he

believed that this was, after all, what he had witnessed. He seemed
tueful, and said that he didn't know what it was that he had seen. Mr.

@ gives the impression that he is really rather embarrassed by it
all, and would rather, frankly, that it hadn't happened to him!

One point that, to my mind, adds to the credibility of the case is that
was unaware of the witnesses at and vice
versa. ertainly, there is no_sign of conspiracy. The eventual arrival

of the sighting details from was
convincing proof to me that an object of some description was seen on

August 3rd.

Section 40 40 seem a_very pleasant couple in their fifties. They
arm on the outskirts of Gftewe@meand list amongst their interests
"anything to do with the countryside, wildlife, etc.". They obviously
went to a great deal of trouble to make their sighting form as accurate
and comprehensive as possible, often clarifying "tick where appropriate"
boxes with extra information. They, too, are intrigued by what they
saw, even going so far as to contact SESE in their quest for an
explanation.

In the wake of these reports, Beteue and I began checking various

possibilities. One point that_we chased up immediately was the
helicopters seen > Se the object had vanished.
Unfortunately (and despite SAxQMCMRACMEM assistance} we have been unable
to ascertain whether or not these were civilian or military.

Soon after the event, I telephoned Air Traffic Control! at RNAS
Yeovilton, this being the nearest military installation to the scene of
the sighting. Although pleasant and helpful, the gentleman I spoke to
was unable to provide me with any information that might explain the
sightings, their records showing no anomalies or UFO reports on the
night in question.

I next contacted various police stations in the area. After consulting
my O.S. maps, I made an estimate of the object's position and concluded
that it was probably somewhere between A ac
Somerset. Accordingly, | initially concentrated upon contacting police
stations in those particular areas. I received a generally helpful
response, but the records for the evening of the 3rd August revealed
nothing that could explain the phenomenon we were investigating. If had
occurred to me that there may have been a laser-show or similar in the

vicinity, but neither I nor the police can find any record of such an
event taking place.

Conclusions

The descriptions of the object given by Bite

are, as stated earlier, intriguingly similar. Having given their

accounts some thought, it occurred to me that what they were describing
sounded remarkably like the description of a cluster of lights upon the
rim of a rotating object, possibly a disc or a sphere. This would both
explain the left-to-right movement of the lights and their periodic

disappearance and reappearance. It would also explain why_the lights
themselves appeared to contract-expand-contract, and why Sijeie@eae
stated that he thought the lights were "moving backwards and forwards".
This, of course, is pure hypothesis, but poses the interesting question:

if the visible lights were part of an unseen object, how big must the
entire object have been?

Not having an accurate idea of distance/height, it is impossible to
ascertain the size of the object. However, I feel it is safe to say

that the sighting involved an object of some considerable size,

certainly more than 10 metres in diameter, and possibly much bigger.
Having checked through my files, I] can find no record of any object with
the same "daisy-pattern" of lights. Although the night of 3rd August
was generally clear and with good visibility, it is conceivabie that the
"petal"-effect may have been caused by rays of light from the light-
source glowing through mist; it is also possible that the object was
generating a mist or vapour that created this visual effect.

It is worth pointing out at this juncture that the initial sighting at
took place on, or very near to, the There has,
of course, always been much speculation about the inctdence of UFO
sightings along this particular ley line. I would personally draw no
conclusions from this, but feel that it is a fact that may be worthy of
note.

To conclude, it is apparent that on the night of 3rd August, 1993, the
area of Biteitearse) was visited by a self-luminous, hovering/slow-
moving object of indeterminate origin. Unless or until further

information comes to hand, it appears that this object must remain
unidentified.

Ninety-five per cent of reports of so-called

the end of the day..

“Some believe that UFOs are ‘nothing

Every year, hundreds of people involved and interested in
the UFO phenomena gather at an international conference
organised by the Yorkshire-based organisation Independent
UFO Network. .

This year the subject of debate at the conference oever the
past weekend was UFOs: Fact, Fraud Or Fantasy? Topics
under discussion ranged from abductions in the USA, UK
and Africa, case histories, hoaxes and theories attempting to
explain the UFO phenomena.

Fascinating

Conference organiser Philip Mantle has been an amateur
UFO investigator for many years. He is one of many investi-
gators from all over the country who belong to the Independ-
ent UFO Network. : :

Last year the organisation opened its doors to public
membership for those wanting to find out more about what
is without doubt a fascinating subject — for believers and
sceptics alike.

Mantle ~~ who works in the traditionally down-to-earth
